OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Bounded Complementary Dual-Channel Algorithm for Constrained Multiobjective Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3755556 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Constrained multiobjective evolutionary algorithms must balance feasibility recovery with objective-space exploration while retaining useful information from infeasible or relaxed search. We propose the Bounded Complementary Dual-Channel Algorithm (BCDCA), which coordinates two concurrently active offspring-generation channels with distinct parent-source roles within a mode-specific search structure. A minimum share keeps both channels active, while feasibility-rate feedback reallocates a conserved controlled offspring quota between them; modes-pecific variation and environmental selection provide the surrounding search process. Experiments on constrained benchmark problems demonstrate competitive performance across diverse constraint geometries. Controlled comparisons show that outcomes vary with parent-source configuration and the tested structural interventions. Experiments on static UAV routing cases also show differences among configurations across map settings, reflecting the interaction between search structure, feasible-region geometry, and objective trade-offs. BCDCA thus provides a bounded dual-channel coordination structure for finite-budget constrained multiobjective search.

Yushuang Hou; Hongfeng Wang; Jinliang Ding

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Learning to Fast-Adapting Optimization: A Meta-Reinforcement Learning-Assisted Memetic Algorithm for Integrated Production-Distribution Scheduling

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3755174 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

The increasing connectivity of manufacturing systems makes the integration of production and distribution crucial for enhancing operational performance. Nevertheless, integrating distributed flexible job shops with vehicle routing creates a highly complex scheduling environment, characterized by intricate couplings among factories, machines, operations, and vehicles. Existing reinforcement learning-assisted evolutionary algorithms often struggle in such settings, as the requirement to learn from scratch hinders the rapid establishment of effective mappings between evolving search states and search operators. To address this limitation, this study introduces a Reptile-based meta-Q-learning-assisted memetic algorithm (MQMA). Through meta-training across multiple optimization processes, a well-initialized Q-table is generated that captures transferable state-operator mapping knowledge. This Q-table provides a favorable starting point for subsequent search and is further fine-tuned according to evolutionary feedback. Thus, the Q-learning model can rapidly identify appropriate search operators in new optimization processes, facilitating fast adaptation under limited computational budgets. In this way, MQMA achieves a superior balance between solution quality and computational efficiency. Extensive experiments on 24 instances, including comparisons with four peers, twelve variants, and CPLEX, verify the superiority of MQMA.

Toward Effective Solution Transfer for Evolutionary Sequential Transfer Optimization

Wu Lin; Weitao Chen; Songbai Liu; Qiuzhen Lin; Liang Feng; Kay Chen Tan

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3755028
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Toward Effective Solution Transfer for Evolutionary Sequential Transfer Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3755028 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

In evolutionary sequential transfer optimization (ESTO), solution transfer can improve the search efficiency of a target task by reusing optimized solutions from previously solved source tasks. However, most existing methods directly use source-to-target task similarity as an indicator of the usefulness of candidate transfer solutions. Such methods do not explicitly model the relationship between task similarity and actual transfer usefulness, potentially leading to redundant solution transfer. To address this issue, this paper proposes an ESTO algorithm with fuzzy classification-based solution transfer, termed ESTOA-FCST. Specifically, similarity features between source and target tasks are first extracted to generate feature vectors for candidate transfer solutions. Next, training samples are continuously constructed by assigning positive or negative labels to feature vectors according to the observed performance of solution transfer. Based on the resulting training and test samples, a fuzzy classifier learns the relationship between similarity features and actual transfer usefulness. The predicted class labels identify potentially useful solutions, while the membership degrees further determine the priorities of positively classified candidates, thereby reducing redundant solution transfer. Experiments on synthetic benchmark problems and practical cases demonstrate the superior performance of ESTOA-FCST over existing methods.

An Automated Evolutionary Algorithm Configuration Framework Based on Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning

Xiaotong Liu; Ye Tian; Shangshang Yang; Yaochu Jin; Xingyi Zhang

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

An Automated Evolutionary Algorithm Configuration Framework Based on Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3753636 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Research on automated evolutionary algorithm configuration has emerged as a key frontier. While learning-assisted configuration offers greater generalization and flexibility than manually designed techniques, the simultaneous adaptation of multiple interdependent components and parameters remains challenging, because their coupling enlarges the configuration space and makes coordinated online decision-making more difficult. To address this challenge, this paper proposes a multi-agent reinforcement learning assisted algorithm configuration framework. The proposed framework enables the automated configuration of multiple key components and parameters in evolutionary algorithms, including operator selection, parameter control, and core component switch. By formulating configuration as a cooperative decision-making process, the framework enables distributed agents to learn policies based on their respective states while coordinating their actions through a shared reward mechanism. This design provides a structured way to handle coupled component and parameter decisions by assigning different configuration responsibilities to cooperative agents. The proposed framework is further applied to automate the configuration of key components and parameters in evolutionary algorithms for sparse and dynamic multi-objective optimization. Experimental results show that the resulting algorithms exhibit improved convergence and adaptability over state-of-the-art methods on both benchmark and real-world problems, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed framework in coordinated multi-component and multi-parameter configuration.

Attainability, Not Dimensionality: Explaining and Exploiting Low-Rank Reformulation in Large-Scale Multiobjective Benchmarks

Yanxiao Li; Yifu Zhao; Junhao Wei; Sio-Kei Im; Xu Yang; Yapeng Wang

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3753834
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Attainability, Not Dimensionality: Explaining and Exploiting Low-Rank Reformulation in Large-Scale Multiobjective Benchmarks

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3753834 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Reduced-basis reformulations such as the weighted optimization framework (WOF) and the large-scale multiobjective optimization framework (LSMOF) can be effective, but basis dimension alone does not explain when they succeed. For the large-scale multiobjective optimization problem (LSMOP) and generalized LSMOP (GLSMOP) constructions, we prove that the convergence-optimal distance block has affine rank two under explicit common-minimizer and feasibility conditions. We define attainability as whether a method’s realized basis can represent this optimal set and quantify it by an offline projection residual. Ten-seed measurements separate LSMOP from GLSMOP for both a proposed role-anchored basis and LSMOF’s basis. The residual difference matches the two methods’ performance ordering on all seven Deb–Thiele–Laumanns–Zitzler (DTLZ) problems (Spearman correlation 0.964) but not on the Walking Fish Group (WFG) problems. Accordingly, REACH combines a position-conditioned, role-anchored reformulation with weight-space and decomposition-recombination members and a learned principal-component subspace for problems without a usable position/distance split. Over 30 runs on LSMOP with 1,000 variables, REACH ranks first by inverted generational distance plus for three and five objectives and second for two, and first by exact hypervolume throughout. On six application-inspired problems, it ranks first by average exact hypervolume, while other baselines lead the DTLZ, WFG, and distance-minimization families.

Feedback-Driven Knowledge-Assisted Differential Evolution for Dynamic Multiobjective Optimization Problems

Si-Cheng Wang; Min-Yi Zheng; Jinghui Zhong; Jun Zhang

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3754571
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Feedback-Driven Knowledge-Assisted Differential Evolution for Dynamic Multiobjective Optimization Problems

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3754571 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Dynamic multiobjective optimization problems (DMOPs) involve multiple conflicting objectives that change over time, leading to the time-varying Pareto-optimal set (PS) and Pareto front (PF). Due to their population-based nature, evolutionary algorithms (EAs) are well-suited for solving DMOPs, yet a major challenge lies in reliably and efficiently tracking the time-varying PS/PF. To address this challenge, most existing EAs follow open-loop paradigms, predicting the updated PS/PF once after each environmental change without feedback or validation. Such open-loop paradigms result in negative transfer and cumulative errors, thereby impairing continuous adaptation to dynamic environments. To overcome these limitations, we propose a feedback-driven knowledge-assisted differential evolution (FDKA-DE) algorithm, which establishes a closed-loop evolutionary paradigm for progressive and adaptive PS tracking. Two core strategies are involved in FDKA-DE. The first is the cluster-based predictive knowledge library building (CPKLB) strategy, which extracts knowledge from historical environments after each environmental change to provide guidance for subsequent tracking of the updated PS. The second is the feedback-driven adaptive knowledge assistance (FDAKA) strategy, which dynamically regulates the migration and utilization of knowledge to ensure reliable continuous tracking. Furthermore, we propose a mean post-change degradation (MPCD) performance measure to quantify the reliability and efficiency of the tracking process after each change. Experiments on standard benchmark suites demonstrate the superior tracking performance of FDKA-DE under various frequencies and severities of environmental changes, where FDKA-DE significantly outperforms six competing algorithms.

Niching-Based Ant Colony Optimization With Multiple Pheromone Matrices for Multi-objective Feature Selection in Classification

Ke Chen; Hansong Hu; Peng Wang; Xiangyang Ren; Kunjie Yu; Jing Liang

卷期
V · I—
门控

DOI

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Niching-Based Ant Colony Optimization With Multiple Pheromone Matrices for Multi-objective Feature Selection in Classification

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3754491 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Feature selection is a typical multi-objective optimization problem, aiming to minimize the number of selected features and the classification error rate simultaneously. However, the complex feature interactions and the high-dimensional search space often lead to premature convergence and a severe loss of population diversity. To address these challenges, this paper proposes a novel multi-objective ant colony optimization algorithm equipped with multiple pheromone matrices for feature selection. In the method, a heuristic-guided solution construction mechanism is developed to incorporate feature relevance and redundancy information into the search process. Then, a niche-based multiple pheromone updating mechanism is introduced, where feature subsets are partitioned into different niches according to their structural similarity. Furthermore, a subset repairing mechanism is incorporated to remove redundant features and enhance the compactness of the obtained solutions. The experiments are conducted on 17 real-world classification datasets and compared against seven state-of-the-art multi-objective feature selection algorithms. The results demonstrate that the proposed method not only evolves a diverse and well-distributed set of non-dominated solutions but also provides decision-makers with flexible feature subset alternatives that achieve competitive classification performance with different levels of feature reduction.

Batch Multi-fidelity Bayesian Optimization via Nonlinear Autoregressive Gaussian Process

Fan Li; Shuang Li; Ziyuan Zhou; Yikun He; Jue Jiang; Ke Li

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3753853
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Batch Multi-fidelity Bayesian Optimization via Nonlinear Autoregressive Gaussian Process

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3753853 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

The realm of expensive optimization applications poses significant challenges due to the high computational costs associated with achieving optimal solutions. Multi-fidelity optimization has emerged as a pivotal approach to mitigate these costs, leveraging the synergy between low-fidelity and high-fidelity problems to enhance optimization efficiency. However, existing methods have limitations in their expressiveness for accurately modeling the complex relationships between fidelity levels, potentially resulting in compromised optimization performance. To addresses these limitations, we introduce a multi-fidelity optimization method that used nonlinear autoregressive Gaussian process regression with composite kernels to better represent the intricate relationships within the data. This allows for more accurate predictions. Furthermore, we introduce a Pareto-based multi-objective sampling strategy for improving model accuracy, where exploration and exploitation are treated as separate objectives. This formulation enables the selection of diverse candidate points from the Pareto set and is further integrated with multiple fidelity management strategies, allowing effective coordination between sampling decisions and fidelity selection. The proposed method is validated on two synthetic benchmark suites and 15 multi-fidelity hyperparameter optimization (HPO) tasks, encompassing 130 test cases in total. Extensive experiments against five state-of-the-art algorithms demonstrate that the proposed method consistently achieves superior performance, finding better solutions in 106 out of 130 cases while reducing computational cost by up to 50%.

Search-to-Learn Memetic Reinforcement Learning for Lot-Streaming Flexible Job Shop Scheduling

Rui Li; Yifan Yang; Ling Wang; Lizhong Yao; Chin Sheng Tan; Xu Chi; Yew-Soon Ong

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Search-to-Learn Memetic Reinforcement Learning for Lot-Streaming Flexible Job Shop Scheduling

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3753459 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

This work studies the lot-streaming flexible job shop scheduling problem (LSFJSP). Conventional meta-heuristics can leverage domain knowledge to generate effective solutions, but often rely on manually designed rules or expensive per-instance search. Deep reinforcement learning (DRL) provides fast online scheduling. However, training a neural policy by RL is challenging, as early-stage policies often generate weak trajectories that provide limited learning signals, resulting in low sample efficiency . To address this issue, this paper proposes a search-to-learn memetic reinforcement learning (S2LMRL) framework for LSFJSP. In S2LMRL, memetic search refines RL-sampled trajectories and returns improved decisions as teacher signals for policy optimization. S2LMRL directly learns a neural scheduling policy as the main solution constructor, while memetic search acts as a training-time teacher. Moreover, the proposed S2LMRL framework is parallelizable, where GPU-based policy learning and CPU-based memetic refinement mutually enhance each other. Experimental results on small-scale training, mid-scale transfer, and cross-scale zero-shot testing show that S2LMRL consistently improves the learned policy over the PureRL baseline. The results also show that reinforcement feedback is necessary for effectively exploiting searched trajectories, and that S2LMRL provides a competitive quality–time tradeoff compared with strong dispatching rules, meta-heuristics, DRL baselines, and symbolic hyper-heuristics.

Class-Aware Evolutionary Fusion Search for Multi-Head Deep Ordinal Decision Making

Che Xu; Leilei Liao; Shaoqiu Zheng; Xuefei Jia; Ying Tan; Peng Zhu

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Class-Aware Evolutionary Fusion Search for Multi-Head Deep Ordinal Decision Making

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3750937 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Ordinal classification problems are prevalent in real-world decision-making scenarios, where class labels are categorical yet inherently ordered. However, existing deep ordinal methods generally adopt a single ordinal modeling paradigm and treat all classes uniformly, making them insufficient for handling heterogeneous ordinal structures and class imbalance. To address these issues, this paper proposes a multi-head deep ordinal decision framework that integrates four representative ordinal paradigms as parallel output heads within a unified architecture, enabling the extraction of complementary ordinal characteristics. The Quadratic Weighted Kappa loss is adopted as a unified training objective, computed independently for each head and averaged to optimize the overall model. After training, the heterogeneous head outputs are combined through a class-aware fusion mechanism, formulated as a single-objective optimization problem that scalarizes the trade-off between prediction error and class-wise diversity. A quality- and similarity-driven differential evolution algorithm, equipped with quality-guided population initialization and similarity-aware mutation, is developed to search for optimal class-wise fusion weights. Experiments on ten public ordinal text classification datasets show that the proposed framework consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods while maintaining robust predictive capability on minority classes.

Lifelong Learning of Scheduling Rules with Surrogate-assisted Genetic Programming for Dynamic Flexible Job Shop Scheduling

Luyao Zhu; Fangfang Zhang; Yi Mei; Ke Chen; Mengjie Zhang

卷期
V · I—
门控

DOI

OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Lifelong Learning of Scheduling Rules with Surrogate-assisted Genetic Programming for Dynamic Flexible Job Shop Scheduling

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3752395 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Genetic programming (GP) has been widely used to learn scheduling rules for dynamic flexible job shop scheduling (DFJSS). However, trained rules tend to only generalise to tasks of similar scales and distributions, and when task characteristics change, learned rules often suffer significant performance degradation. Moreover, fine-tuning pre-trained rules on new tasks often leads to catastrophic forgetting, where prior knowledge is lost. This motivates the need for GP methods that can adapt to new tasks while preserving past knowledge, leading to a lifelong learning paradigm. However, lifelong learning in GP for DFJSS remains largely under-explored. To address this gap, this paper proposes a surrogate-assisted GP method for lifelong learning to handle sequential heterogeneous DFJSS tasks with job or batch arrivals. The framework includes three key components: an adaptive two-stage training strategy to balance plasticity and stability, a task-similarity-based weighting mechanism to regulate knowledge reuse, and an improved surrogate model with distance constraints to enhance estimation accuracy. Experimental results show that the proposed method effectively mitigates catastrophic forgetting while maintaining strong adaptability, outperforming several compared methods and demonstrating the potential of lifelong GP in dynamic scheduling environments.

EvoDR: Evolving Dispatching Rules via Large Language Model for Dynamic Flexible Assembly Flow Shop Scheduling

Junhao Qiu; Haoyang Zhuang; Fei Liu; Jianjun Liu; Qingfu Zhang

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3751778
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

EvoDR: Evolving Dispatching Rules via Large Language Model for Dynamic Flexible Assembly Flow Shop Scheduling

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3751778 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Dynamic flexible assembly flow shop scheduling with multi-product delivery, often with kitting supply and machine-level flexibility, can be found in many applications. It is difficult to design rules manually for it. Genetic programming (GP) has been used to automatically generate dispatching rules, enabling responsive scheduling while meeting high responsiveness demands. However, GP methods are dependent on fixed terminal sets and have poor interpretability. In this work, we develop an evolving dispatching rules framework (EvoDR) that leverages the semantic understanding and generation capabilities of large language models to achieve cross-domain integration of rule design and scheduling knowledge. In our proposed framework, multi-stage assembly supply decisions are modeled as priority sorting of directed edges based on heterogeneous graphs. A dual-expert co-evolution mechanism is proposed, which allows LLM-A to generate code and LLM-S conduct scheduling analysis and reflection. Guided by improvements in hybrid evaluation, adaptive rules that fit dynamic features are continuously evolved. Experimental results show that EvoDR can achieve lower average tardiness than other automatically designed state-of-the-art approaches. It also outperforms expert-designed competitors across 480 instances over 24 distinct scenarios with varying resource configurations and disturbance levels, and EvoDR demonstrates robust adaptability across diverse operating conditions.

Automatic Design of Differential Evolution Optimizers for Surrogate-Assisted Evolutionary Algorithms via Large Language Models

Libin Lin; Wei Huang; Lijun Wei; Jiewu Leng; Weiming Xu; Qiang Liu; Tao Zou

卷期
V · I—
门控

DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3751216
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Automatic Design of Differential Evolution Optimizers for Surrogate-Assisted Evolutionary Algorithms via Large Language Models

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3751216 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Surrogate-assisted evolutionary algorithms (SAEAs) are widely used to tackle expensive optimization problems (EOPs), and their optimization performance largely depends on the effectiveness and robustness of the evolutionary search process. However, most existing SAEAs still rely on manually designed parameter and operator adaptation strategies, which are problem-dependent and require substantial expert knowledge. This paper proposes a large language model-assisted differential evolution optimizers automatic design (LLM-DOAD) framework to improve both the design efficiency of SAEAs and their performance in solving EOPs. Unlike redesigning surrogate model management, this paper focuses on automatically designing a differential evolution optimizer via LLMs. Specifically, problem-oriented prompts for EOPs guide LLMs to construct two essential modules, a parameter adaptation mechanism based on hybrid fitness improvement and dimension improvement and a multi-criteria and multi-stage operator adaptation mechanism. Moreover, a self-evolution learning approach is proposed to enhance the capability of LLMs in algorithm design and improve the quality of the generated algorithms. Finally, an LLM-based surrogate-assisted improved multi-operator differential evolution (LSIMD) algorithm is developed. Experiments on benchmark problems comprising 50, 100, and 200 dimensions show that LSIMD substantially outperforms several state-of-the-art manually designed algorithms, demonstrating the effectiveness and potential of LLM-DOAD for expensive optimization. LSIMD code is available at https://github.com/dabin64/LSIMD.git.

PF-SAEA: A Probabilistic Feasibility-Guided Surrogate-Assisted Evolutionary Algorithm for Expensive Constrained Multiobjective Optimisation

Zheng Cai; Yit Hong Choo; Tao Zhou; Vu Le; Wei Hong Lim; Chee Peng Lim

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3752093
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

PF-SAEA: A Probabilistic Feasibility-Guided Surrogate-Assisted Evolutionary Algorithm for Expensive Constrained Multiobjective Optimisation

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3752093 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Surrogate-assisted evolutionary algorithms (SAEAs) have emerged as a promising way to reduce the computational cost of expensive constrained multiobjective optimisation problems (ECMOPs). However, under limited evaluation budgets, surrogate uncertainty near constraint boundaries may be amplified by inconsistent search decisions, thereby misleading the search and wasting expensive function evaluations. To address this issue, this paper proposes a probabilistic feasibility-guided (PF-Guided) framework that uses shared constraint-satisfaction probabilities to guide surrogate-population replacement and infill selection. Uncertainty-weighted global-local constraint modelling produces fused constraint-satisfaction probabilities for each candidate solution. The probability-vector constrained dominance principle (PV-CDP) uses the minimum and geometric mean of these probabilities across constraints for population replacement. Probability-feasible infill selection reuses the minimum constraint-satisfaction probability and feasibility threshold to select candidates for real evaluation. The PF-Guided framework is embedded in a two-stage SAEA, termed PF-SAEA, which combines unconstrained exploration with probability-guided constrained search. Experiments on 37 benchmark instances from the MW, DAS-CMOP, and LIRCMOP suites and six real-world engineering benchmark problems show that PF-SAEA achieves strong overall performance compared with seven representative peer algorithms and remains competitive on application-oriented ECMOPs.

An Evolutionary Adversarial Deep Q-Network for Real-time Distributed Hybrid Flow-shop Scheduling with Limited Transportation Resources

Yuanzhu Di; Libao Deng; Chunlei Li; Ning Fu; Hongyan Sang

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

An Evolutionary Adversarial Deep Q-Network for Real-time Distributed Hybrid Flow-shop Scheduling with Limited Transportation Resources

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3751303 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Modern distributed manufacturing suffers from complicated scheduling difficulties under constrained transportation resources and real-time dynamic disruptions. This paper addresses the dynamic distributed hybrid flow-shop scheduling problem with limited transportation (DDHFSP/LT) to optimize makespan and total energy consumption (TEC). An evolutionary adversarial deep Q-network (EA-DQN) is proposed, which constructs two collaborative agents for machine and AGV selection, respectively. The two sub-decision tasks are formulated as Markov decision processes. By introducing an adversarial learning mechanism, the agents absorb high-quality scheduling knowledge from expert algorithms and are optimized via DQN. Ablation tests and comparative experiments on static and multiple dynamic scenarios (new job arrivals, machine breakdowns, AGV breakdowns) show that EA-DQN achieves the minimum makespan across all test instances. Although competing algorithms obtain marginally better TEC on partial cases, EA-DQN yields superior overall performance by balancing the two conflicting objectives.

Simple but Difficult Multi-objective Distance Minimization Problems with Disconnected Global and Local Pareto Sets

Tianye Shu; Yang Nan; Longcan Chen; Lie Meng Pang; Hisao Ishibuchi

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3749967
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Simple but Difficult Multi-objective Distance Minimization Problems with Disconnected Global and Local Pareto Sets

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3749967 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Evolutionary multi-objective optimization algorithms (EMOAs) aim to approximate the Pareto front with good convergence and diversity. While most existing EMOAs emphasize objective-space diversity, they largely neglect decision-space diversity. This limitation can lead to incomplete exploration of disconnected Pareto set regions. To analyze this phenomenon, this paper proposes a class of distance minimization problems, termed the generalized polygon test problems. These problems exhibit disconnected global and local Pareto sets and retain a clear geometric structure for visualizing search behavior. We compare seven standard EMOAs and seven multi-modal EMOAs. On three-objective problems with high-dimensional decision spaces, several multi-modal EMOAs outperform standard EMOAs when the evaluation budget is sufficiently large. This advantage is associated with their ability to identify more disconnected Pareto set regions. However, on problems with low-dimensional decision spaces or many objectives, several standard EMOAs, especially those with diversity-enhancement mechanisms, achieve competitive or superior performance. Further analysis indicates that multi-modal EMOAs preserve decision-space diversity well, but this may weaken objective-space convergence. Results obtained with an external archive show that archive-based subset selection can improve objective-space performance, particularly for multi-modal EMOAs. These findings highlight the benefits of decision-space diversity and external archives on the generalized polygon test problems.

Evolutionary Community Detection Attack via Community-Aware Graph Representation Learning

Haipeng Yang; Fuwu Liu; Fan Cheng; Yuanyuan Ge; Lei Zhang

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3749401
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Evolutionary Community Detection Attack via Community-Aware Graph Representation Learning

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3749401 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Community detection attack (CDA) aims to obfuscate the community structure of networks through constrained edge modifications, helping mitigate community-related privacy risks. Among existing studies, evolutionary algorithms (EAs) have demonstrated strong capabilities in exploring effective perturbation strategies. However, existing EA-based CDA methods suffer from high computational costs of individual evaluation due to repeated invocation of complex community detection algorithms, limiting their applicability to large-scale networks. In addition, the combinatorial explosion of possible edge modifications leads to an excessively large search space. To address these issues, we propose a community-aware graph representation learning (GRL)-driven evolutionary algorithm for CDA, termed CDA-CAGRL. The algorithm extracts global graph and local node representations via community-aware GRL and explicitly integrates them into the evolutionary search process to achieve efficient perturbation optimization. First, we suggest a community-aware graph representation-based individual evaluation (CGI) strategy, which quickly captures community structure via GRL, avoiding repeated use of traditional community detection algorithms. Second, we design a community-aware node representation-based offspring generation (CNO) operator, which defines gene strength via node embedding similarity and introduces a strength-biased mechanism in crossover and mutation, thereby improving search efficiency. Extensive experiments on 12 real-world networks demonstrate significant improvements in both attack effectiveness and computational efficiency.

Multiobjective Stackelberg Security Games: Problem Formulation, Benchmarks and Evolutionary Algorithms

Adam Żychowski; Abhishek Gupta; Yew-Soon Ong; Jacek Mańdziuk

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3749205
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Multiobjective Stackelberg Security Games: Problem Formulation, Benchmarks and Evolutionary Algorithms

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3749205 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

This paper introduces a comprehensive framework for Multiobjective Stackelberg Security Games (MO-SSGs), extending traditional single-objective formulations to settings where both the defender (Leader) and attacker (Follower) optimize vector-valued payoffs. We define equilibrium concepts for these games and establish their key properties. A hallmark of the MO-SSG formulation is the explicit use of mixed strategies by the Leader—defined as a probability distribution over pure strategies—to confuse the Follower. Another crucial feature is that a single Leader strategy can yield a set of vector-valued payoffs due to the existence of multiple optimal Follower responses. To address this strategy-to-payoff set mapping, we introduce the concept of worst-case dominance, a pessimistic approach for evaluating strategies against adversarial Followers. This formulation precludes direct application of standard Pareto-based Multiobjective Evolutionary Algorithms (MOEAs). Consequently, we propose two specialized population-based search methods: the Worst-Case Dominance Evolutionary Algorithm (WCD-EA) and the Worst-Case Dominance Coevolutionary Algorithm (WCD-CoEA). These algorithms utilize novel evolutionary selection and variation operators to facilitate convergence toward worst-case nondominated strategy sets while preserving diversity in the Leader’s multidimensional objective space. We validate our approach using a new benchmark suite of instructional and practically-inspired MO-SSG instances. Experimental results demonstrate the computational feasibility of the framework and characterize the trade-offs between solution quality and efficiency. Ultimately, this work demonstrates that the unique landscape of MO-SSGs calls for the design of new evolutionary methods and operators, offering a foundation for future research into multiobjective sequential defender–attacker models.

Any Constant Mutation Rate Can be Optimal for the (1+1) Evolutionary Algorithm

Andrew James Kelley

卷期

门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3748360
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Any Constant Mutation Rate Can be Optimal for the (1+1) Evolutionary Algorithm

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3748360 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

For every mutation rate p ∈ (0, 1), for all ε > 0, there is a fitness function f : {0, 1} n → R with a unique maximum for which the optimal mutation rate for the (1 + 1) EA on f is in ( p − ε, p + ε). In other words, the set of optimal mutation rates for the (1+1) EA is dense in the interval [0, 1]. To show that, this paper introduces DISTANTSTEPPINGSTONES p , a fitness function which consists of large plateaus separated by large fitness valleys. This fitness function is a function of unitation (meaning that it depends only on the number of 1s of the individual, rather than on which bits are 1).

Modelling and Analysis of Dynamics of Particle Swarm Optimization under Time-Varying Inertia using Green-Sturm-Liouville System

Krishnendu Biswas; Shyamali Mitra; Tamal Panda; Nibaran Das

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3748311
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Modelling and Analysis of Dynamics of Particle Swarm Optimization under Time-Varying Inertia using Green-Sturm-Liouville System

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3748311 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Particle Swarm Optimization (PSO) is typically analyzed using stochastic difference equations and parameter-based stability criteria under constant or piecewise-constant inertia assumptions. Although such approaches provide stability bounds, they offer limited trajectory-level insight into particle dynamics under time-varying inertia. This work formulates the expected PSO dynamics as a discrete Sturm–Liouville system. Under a linearized stagnation assumption, the proposed framework yields closed-form trajectory solutions for a broad class of continuous time-varying inertia functions, enabling analytical characterization of oscillatory, convergent, and divergent regimes. To relax the strict stagnation assumption, Green’s function theory for non-homogeneous Sturm–Liotruville difference equations is incorporated to model forced dynamics induced by moving attractors. Validation using swarm-level metrics, including Swarm Diameter and Swarm Coherence, shows strong agreement between theoretical and simulated trajectories. The framework analytically predicts optimization trajectories without computationally intensive simulations. As a case study, the model is applied to EBPSO and CGPSO variants with time-varying inertia on benchmark feature-selection problems. Theoretical predictions closely match empirical convergence behavior across 10 UCI datasets and 4 high-dimensional datasets (> 1000 features).

Harnessing Discrete Heterogeneous Spatial Organisation for Structural Topology Optimisation

Edgar Buchanan; Simon Hickinbotham; Imelda Friel; Paul Goodall; Ge Shi; David Howard; Mark Price; Andy M. Tyrrell

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3747550
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Harnessing Discrete Heterogeneous Spatial Organisation for Structural Topology Optimisation

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3747550 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Morphogenesis in biology demonstrates how spatial organisation and material heterogeneity can be exploited to achieve high levels of functionality and efficiency. In contrast, conventional engineering design and topology optimisation methods often favour homogeneous material distributions, limiting their ability to exploit heterogeneity for multi-objective performance. Incorporating spatially varying material properties into evolutionary design remains challenging due to the complexity of encoding and managing heterogeneous structures. This paper introduces Spatial Organisation Representations (SORs) as a unified framework for encoding material distribution and heterogeneity within evolutionary topology optimisation. SORs provide compact, locality-preserving encodings that define discrete material layouts upon decoding, enabling efficient exploration of heterogeneous design spaces. A range of SORs are systematically benchmarked in target replication tasks, the top-performing representations are subsequently evaluated on complex mechanical configurations. The results demonstrate that SORs consistently produce structurally coherent and mechanically efficient designs while requiring shorter genome lengths and fewer evaluations than explicit representations. Moreover, locality-preserving SORs exhibit improved scalability and reduced imperfections associated with disconnected or inefficient material layouts. Overall, this work illustrates that SORs offer a biologically inspired and computationally efficient approach to evolutionary topology optimisation, bridging principles from developmental biology with engineering design practice and enabling scalable optimisation of discrete heterogeneous structures.

IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · ONLINE_FIRST

An Evolutionary Algorithm with Probabilistic Annealing for Large-scale Sparse Multi-objective Optimization

Shuai Shao; Ye Tian; Guan Wang; Jin Li; Yuhao Sun; Xing Chen

论文题名

An Evolutionary Algorithm with Probabilistic Annealing for Large-scale Sparse Multi-objective Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3745886 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Large-scale sparse multi-objective optimization problems (LSMOPs) are prevalent in real-world applications, where optimal solutions typically contain only a few nonzero variables, such as in adversarial attacks, critical node detection, and sparse signal reconstruction. Since the function evaluation of LSMOPs often relies on large-scale datasets involving a large number of decision variables, the search space becomes extremely high-dimensional. The coexistence of sparsity and high dimensionality greatly intensifies the conflict between exploration and exploitation, making it difficult for existing multi-objective evolutionary algorithms (MOEAs) to identify the critical nonzero decision variables within limited function evaluations. To address this challenge, this paper proposes an evolutionary algorithm with probabilistic annealing for large-scale sparse multi-objective optimization. The algorithm is driven by two complementary probability vectors: a convergence-oriented probability vector that provides stable guidance for exploitation, and an annealed probability vector that gradually adjusts the degree of search uncertainty during evolution, enabling an adaptive transition from global exploration to local refinement. By integrating these complementary search dynamics, the proposed algorithm achieves a dynamic equilibrium between exploration and exploitation. Experimental results on benchmark problems and real-world applications demonstrate that the proposed algorithm outperforms state-of-the-art evolutionary algorithms in terms of both convergence and diversity.

Evolutionary Graph Neural Architecture Search for Heterogeneous Graph-Based Combinatorial Optimization

Nan Li; Yuhua Qian; Aohan Mei; Tian Zhang; Ruwang Jiao; Cuie Yang; Lianbo Ma; Yuhui Shi

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Evolutionary Graph Neural Architecture Search for Heterogeneous Graph-Based Combinatorial Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3745054 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Graph neural networks (GNNs) provide a powerful learning paradigm for combinatorial optimization (CO). However, many real-world CO problems involve multiple entity and relation types, whereas existing approaches predominantly use manually designed architectures or target homogeneous graphs. To address this limitation, we propose an evolutionary graph neural architecture search framework for heterogeneous graph-based CO, termed EHG-CO. The framework constructs a heterogeneity-aware search space combining flexible topologies with heterogeneous aggregation operations. A node-wise edge-pair encoding jointly represents architectural topology and edge-specific aggregation operations, supporting compact representation and evolutionary optimization. To reduce architecture evaluation costs, an online multi-level rank-based predictor estimates candidate quality, while a rank-aware uncertainty filling strategy selects informative and promising candidates for actual evaluation and predictor refinement. Experiments on six benchmark heterogeneous graph-based CO problems demonstrate that EHG-CO achieves superior solution quality and competitive search efficiency compared with representative methods. Ablation studies further confirm the effectiveness of the search space, encoding scheme, predictor, and filling strategy.

Ying Bi; Jinze Chen; Caitong Yue; Jing Liang

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

An Improved Evolutionary Algorithm with Joint Encoding for Multiobjective Feature Selection in Classification

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3744732 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Feature selection is a complex multiobjective optimization problem that requires balancing the number of selected features and classification performance. Redundant features and complex feature interactions pose significant challenges for traditional multiobjective feature selection algorithms. Existing methods typically employ either discrete or continuous encoding schemes, both of which have inherent limitations, i.e., discrete encoding struggles to capture complex feature interactions, while continuous encoding incurs high computational costs when handling redundant features. To address these issues, this paper proposes a new evolutionary multiobjective feature selection algorithm with a joint encoding mechanism, incorporating a novel joint encoding region partitioning method, an effective initialization strategy, and a new search strategy. Specifically, the joint encoding region partitioning method groups features into discrete or continuous search regions based on their importance and label relevance. An effective initialization strategy is designed to generate high-quality initial solutions in both regions. In addition, a new search strategy dynamically adjusts the number of features in discrete search regions to improve exploration. Experimental results on 16 datasets show that the proposed approach surpasses nine evolutionary multiobjective feature selection algorithms. The proposed approach effectively enhances population diversity while achieving a better balance between the number of selected features and classification performance.

Q-Learning Enhanced Multi-objective Evolutionary Algorithm with Diverse Search for Airport Ground Movement Optimization

Junting Yang; Yi Mei; Tong Guo; Songwei Liu; Jun Chen; Hui Ding; Wenbo Du

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Q-Learning Enhanced Multi-objective Evolutionary Algorithm with Diverse Search for Airport Ground Movement Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3744729 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Airport ground movement (AGM) optimization plays a crucial role in improving the operational efficiency of airport surface systems. The multi-objective multigraph model has been developed to optimize taxi time and fuel consumption with variable speed profiles. However, existing studies based on this model optimize aircraft sequentially under first-come-first-served principles, which inherently restricts the attainable solution space of the entire fleet. To overcome this limitation, we propose a global multi-objective multigraph AGM model that simultaneously optimizes all aircraft. This global formulation results in a high-dimensional combinatorial optimization problem with tightly coupled spatiotemporal constraints. Therefore, we develop a Q-learning-enhanced multi-objective evolutionary algorithm. First, a heuristic-guided decoding procedure incorporating topological information is designed to reduce path infeasibility, and a spatiotemporal repair mechanism is further developed to recover the remaining infeasible solutions. Second, four mating pools are designed with different parent partition schemes and variation operators to explore different search directions in the high-dimensional space. Finally, a Q-learning-based mechanism is developed to adaptively select active mating pools at each generation based on population diversity and convergence status, thereby concentrating computational resources on the most effective search directions. On most of the 16 real-world instances, MPRA-QL outperforms state-of-the-art methods in hypervolume and inverted generational distance.

Topology-Adaptive Evolutionary Multiobjective Optimization for Black-Box Adversarial Attacks on Large Audio-Language Models

Wencheng Han; Hao Li; Maoguo Gong; Yue Wu; Yu Zhou; Lining Xing

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Topology-Adaptive Evolutionary Multiobjective Optimization for Black-Box Adversarial Attacks on Large Audio-Language Models

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3744149 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

The widespread adoption of Large Audio-Language Models (LALMs) under black-box, Model-as-a-Service (MaaS) paradigms has heightened concerns regarding their safety, especially against adversarial attacks that manipulate input audio with optimized prefixes. Identifying effective prefixes is a high-dimensional constrained multiobjective optimization problem characterized by sparse feasible regions and unavailable gradients. Existing methods face challenges in this landscape due to the high-dimensional search space and representational limitations. Fixed-length representations could not adapt to instance-dependent topological complexity, while conventional constraint handling often converges prematurely in deceptive infeasible regions. To address these challenges, this paper proposes a Topology-Adaptive Hierarchical Evolutionary framework. First, a Hierarchical Sparse Generative Representation for Mel-spectrograms projects the search onto a structured low-dimensional manifold. Second, Adaptive Gaussian Mixture Model (AdaGMM) enables integrated structural-parametric evolution through dynamic split-and-merge operations, adaptively matching representation complexity to latent vulnerability structures. Third, we propose Multiobjective Topology-Adaptive Evolution (MO-TAE), an evolutionary solver that combines feasibility-driven hierarchical ranking with constraint-relaxed Pareto guidance to steer populations toward sparse feasible boundaries while balancing attack effectiveness and perceptual stealth. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed framework achieves competitive performance in both attack success rate and perceptual quality compared with state-of-the-art multiobjective methods.

Dynamics-Aware Heuristic Design: Generating Efficient and Specialized Solvers via Large Language Models

Rongzheng Wang; Yihong Huang; Muquan Li; Jiakai Li; Di Liang; Bob Simons; Pei Ke; Shuang Liang; Ke Qin

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Dynamics-Aware Heuristic Design: Generating Efficient and Specialized Solvers via Large Language Models

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3743541 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Large language models have advanced combinatorial optimization through automated heuristic generation. This process replaces manual design by iteratively generating and refining heuristic solvers to achieve high performance. However, existing frameworks face two limitations: (1) Endpoint-only evaluation, which ranks solvers by final gap, ignoring convergence progress and runtime efficiency; (2) High adaptation costs, where shifts in instance profiles, such as problem scale, density, and constraints, degrade existing solvers and require restarting language model search to obtain group-specific specialists. To address these issues, we propose a dynamics-aware framework that co-optimizes search mechanisms and runtime schedules guided by a convergence-aware metric. To reduce re-adaptation, the framework incorporates Profiled Library Retrieval, which maintains group-specialized solvers for profile-aware warm starts. These solvers are archived concurrently, allowing the framework to reuse matched specialists across heterogeneous distributions without restarting adaptation. Experiments on four combinatorial optimization problems demonstrate that the framework improves runtime efficiency by more than four times while outperforming prior automated heuristic design baselines in balancing gap and runtime across scales. By enabling profile-aware warm starts, the framework maintains lower gap under these shifts while reducing repeated language model adaptation costs by about 90 percent.

A Strategic-Tactical Evolutionary Reinforcement Learning Framework for Robotic Mobile Fulfillment Systems

Xiaoying Yang; Fuhua Jia; Tianxiang Cui; Jianfeng Ren; Ruibin Bai

卷期
V · I—
门控

10.1109/tevc.2026.3740457
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

A Strategic-Tactical Evolutionary Reinforcement Learning Framework for Robotic Mobile Fulfillment Systems

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3740457 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Robotic Mobile Fulfillment Systems (RMFS) are widely deployed in e-commerce warehouses, where fleets of autonomous mobile robots transport inventory pods to picking and replenishment workstations. Reliable large-scale RMFS control remains difficult because workstation-role configurations, pod availability, AMR traffic, and time-varying orders are tightly coupled, and workstation role reconfiguration forms a sparse-reward combinatorial decision problem over long strategic horizons. To address these challenges, we propose a Strategic-Tactical Evolutionary Reinforcement Learning framework (STERL) for joint long-term resource reallocation and short-term task execution. The framework formulates RMFS operation as a hierarchical semi-Markov game with two cooperative agents on asynchronous timescales: a strategic agent reallocates workstation roles over long horizons, and a tactical agent performs real-time AMR dispatching, picking- and replenishment-station selection, and pod repositioning under the current strategic configuration. To efficiently train this complex dual-agent system, STERL builds a bidirectional closed loop: the tactical policy and critic provide fast surrogate evaluations through feasibility-aware execution signals for evolutionary strategic search, while the evolutionary population returns diverse elite configurations and marginal pseudo-rewards that densify strategic feedback and broaden tactical training coverage. The pseudo-reward is used as a training-time shaping signal with an annealed weight, and the theoretical analysis provides explicit error terms for surrogate evaluation, rolling-buffer approximation, tactical augmentation, and strategic–tactical coupling. Experiments on public RMFS benchmarks and the RAWSim-O simulator across multiple warehouse scales and nonstationary demand patterns demonstrate superior cost efficiency, scalability across tested layouts, and robustness compared with rule-based policies, analytical baselines, and representative learning-based methods.

Qingling Zhu; Ziyang Hu; Qiuzhen Lin; Carlos A. Coello Coello; Junfa Mao; Jianqiang Li

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

An Evolutionary Reinforcement Learning Framework for Macro Placement Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3740954 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

In modern electronic design automation systems, macro placement optimization is critical for improving chip performance while reducing manufacturing costs. Reinforcement Learning (RL) has shown promising potential in automating macro placement without human intervention, but its inherently low sample efficiency restricts effective exploration in large state spaces and increases the risk of convergence to local optima. To overcome these limitations, this paper presents ERLPlace, a novel framework that integrates the global exploration capability of Evolutionary Algorithms (EAs) with the local exploitation strength of RL to automatically generate high-quality overlap-free macro placements. ERLPlace provides several distinct advantages compared to previous approaches. First, global search capacity is enhanced by performing crossover between the population and elite agents, which introduces diverse candidate solutions for RL and facilitates broader exploration to avoid local optima. Underperforming individuals are eliminated and rolled back to the prior states, mitigating randomness and improving robustness. Second, local search capacity is enhanced by RL, which leverages gradient-based updates to iteratively refine agent policies. Third, EA and RL are tightly coupled through a shared replay buffer and update mechanism. This way, agents in RL can effectively leverage collective experience from the EA during the training process, facilitating that it can escape from suboptimal solutions. Comprehensive experimental evaluations conducted on public benchmarks demonstrate that ERLPlace outperforms existing RL and EA-based methods in macro placement optimization.

Hierarchical Optimization Framework for Overlapping Large-Scale Optimization Problems

Yunpeng Jing; Hai-Lin Liu

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Hierarchical Optimization Framework for Overlapping Large-Scale Optimization Problems

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3740965 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Decomposition methods are widely employed in Large-Scale Global Optimization (LSGO), particularly when the objective function exhibits separability. The traditional Cooperative Co-evolution (CC) framework, which decomposes high-dimensional separable functions into multiple low-dimensional sub-problems, has gained extensive application due to its ability to reduce search space dimensionality while preserving solution equivalence. However, handling overlapping variables within the CC framework remains a challenging task. Due to the inherent incompatibility between function non-separability and the separability assumption of the CC framework, traditional methods struggle to either guarantee equivalence with the original problem or effectively reduce the search space dimension. To address this issue, this paper extends the concept of variable separability to Conditional Variable Separability and introduces the Variable Separation Tree (VST) to formally transform the original problem into an equivalent, multi-layered optimization problem. Building on this, this paper proposes a novel computational framework, termed the HO framework. Theoretical analysis validates that the new multi-layered optimization problem is equivalent to the original problem while effectively reducing the dimensionality of the search space. Numerical experiments demonstrate that the algorithm derived from the core principles of this framework achieves performance comparable to state-of-the-art methods on linearly overlapping problems, and shows clear advantages on more complex problems.

论文题名

Parking Vehicle-Assisted Task Offloading in Edge Computing: An Environment-Adaptive Multi-Strategy Dynamic Multi-Objective Evolutionary Algorithm

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3740890 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Vehicle edge computing (VEC) supports low-latency, computation-intensive vehicular applications. To address the challenges of limited computational resources and suboptimal task offloading in the Internet of Vehicles (IoV) environment, this paper proposes a service architecture that integrates edge servers (ESs) with the resources of parked vehicles (PVs). By grouping PVs into parked vehicle clusters (PVCs) alongside roadside units (RSUs) as service nodes, the coverage of edge computing is extended. Tasks are modeled as directed acyclic graphs (DAGs) to capture dependencies, and the offloading problem is formulated as a dynamic multi-objective optimization (DMOP) that minimizes both average computational latency (ACL) and average energy consumption (AEC). To solve this problem, we propose a transformer-based, environmentadaptive, multi-strategy dynamic multi-objective evolutionary algorithm (DMOEA/D-TBEAMS). The algorithm introduces a multi-dimensional environmental change perception mechanism and a multi-strategy fusion response mechanism, which adaptively adjusts the response according to the degree of change. In the static optimization phase, the Pareto adaptive scalarizing method (PaS) is used to balance convergence and diversity. Simulation results show that compared to mainstream algorithms, the proposed algorithm achieves faster convergence speed, better solution set distribution, and improved stability in dynamic task offloading scenarios, demonstrating an effective trade-off between latency and energy consumption.

Knowledge- and Learning-Driven Artificial Bee Colony Optimization for Home Health Care Routing and Scheduling Problems

论文题名

Knowledge- and Learning-Driven Artificial Bee Colony Optimization for Home Health Care Routing and Scheduling Problems

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3737501 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Recent years have witnessed increasing pressure on public healthcare resources due to population aging, highlighting the importance of healthcare for the elderly as a critical issue. Home health care is emerging as a promising alternative to public hospital treatment. This work for the first time proposes a home health care routing and scheduling problem by considering caregiver available time slots as well as customer requirements regarding specific service time windows and caregiver skill levels. First, a mixed integer programming model is formulated to minimize operation costs and weighted penalties incurred by earliness and tardiness service. Second, a knowledge- and learning-driven artificial bee colony optimization algorithm is specially developed. At the employed bee phase, a problem-specific route-based crossover operation is introduced, and a multi-start simulated annealing method based on proximal policy optimization is designed for the onlooker bee phase. In addition, an individual improvement strategy by integrating dominated properties is elaborately designed. Finally, extensive experiments are conducted by comparing the developed model and algorithm with an exact solver CPLEX and three state-of-the-art multi-objective meta-heuristics. The verification results and analysis confirm their excellent competitiveness for handling the considered problems.

Fairness-Aware Performance Evaluation for Multi-Party Multi-Objective Optimization

Zifan Zhao; Peilan Xu; Wenjian Luo

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3739992
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Fairness-Aware Performance Evaluation for Multi-Party Multi-Objective Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3739992 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

In multi-party multi-objective optimization problems (MPMOPs), solution sets are commonly evaluated using classical performance indicators, such as inverted generational distance (IGD) and hypervolume (HV), aggregated across decision makers (DMs). However, such mean-based evaluations may implicitly favor certain parties by assuming comparable geometric approximation quality across heterogeneous party-wise Pareto fronts (PFs). Moreover, prevailing notions of MPMOP optimality are largely restricted to strictly common Pareto-optimal solutions, which represent a narrow and sometimes impractical form of cooperation. These limitations obscure whether a solution set reflects balanced relative gains or meaningful consensus among heterogeneous DMs. To address these issues, this paper develops a fairness-aware performance evaluation framework grounded in a generalized notion of consensus solutions. From a cooperative game-theoretic perspective, we formalize four axioms for fairness-aware MPMOP evaluation. By introducing a concession rate vector to quantify acceptable compromises, we generalize the classical definition of MPMOP optimal solutions and embed classical performance metrics into a Nash-product-based evaluation framework, which is theoretically shown to satisfy all four proposed axioms. To support empirical validation, we construct benchmark problems with consensus-deficient negotiation structures. The empirical study covers MPMOP instances with up to three DMs and up to five objectives per DM. Experimental results show that, compared with classical indicators such as meanIGD and meanHV, the proposed framework distinguishes algorithmic performance consistently with consensus-aware fairness considerations. Algorithms converging toward strictly common solutions receive higher scores when such solutions exist, whereas algorithms effectively covering commonly acceptable regions are favored when strict common solutions are absent.

Meta-Learning-Guided Deep Kernel Gaussian Process for High-Dimensional Dynamic Expensive Optimization

Huan Zhang; Jinliang Ding; Liang Feng; Zhenzhong Wang; Kay Chen Tan

卷期

门控

10.1109/tevc.2026.3740007

OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Meta-Learning-Guided Deep Kernel Gaussian Process for High-Dimensional Dynamic Expensive Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3740007 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

High-dimensional dynamic expensive optimization problems are characterized by high dimensionality, costly objective evaluations, and shifting optima, all of which pose significant coupled challenges in the field of optimization. To address these challenges simultaneously, this paper proposes a novel evolutionary algorithm, ML-DKGPEA, which integrates meta-learning with a deep kernel Gaussian process (DKGP). Specifically, the algorithm employs a DKGP surrogate to construct compact spatial representations by projecting the high-dimensional decision space into a learned latent manifold, thereby mitigating the curse of dimensionality. When the environment changes, a Bayesian meta-learning mechanism facilitates rapid temporal adaptation . It transfers the learned deep-kernel parameters from historical dynamics to warm-start the surrogate model and generates a high-quality initial population for the new environment. By synergizing the spatial reduction capability of DKGP with the temporal knowledge transfer of meta-learning, the proposed algorithm is able to efficiently track the time-varying optimum in a high-dimensional space, even under a strictly limited computational budget. Experimental studies on 11 benchmark test problems and a real-world application validate that ML-DKGPEA outperforms four state-of-the-art algorithms in terms of both effectiveness and efficiency.

Automatic Design of Optimization Test Problems with Large Language Models

Wojciech Achtelik; Hubert Guzowski; Maciej Smołka; Jacek Mańdziuk

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Automatic Design of Optimization Test Problems with Large Language Models

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3740030 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Diverse and representative benchmark suites are essential for designing robust optimization algorithms capable of solving complex black-box problems. Widely used collections, such as the black-box optimization benchmarking (BBOB) test suite, remain dominated by hand-crafted synthetic functions and provide limited coverage of the high-dimensional space of Exploratory Landscape Analysis (ELA) features, which in turn biases evaluation and hinders training of meta-black-box optimizers. We introduce Evolution of Test Functions (EoTF), a framework that automatically generates continuous optimization test functions whose landscapes match a specified target ELA feature vector. EoTF adapts evolutionary search guided by Large Language Models (LLMs), originally proposed for heuristic discovery, to evolve interpretable, self-contained Python/NumPy implementations of objective functions by minimizing the distance between sampled ELA features of generated candidates and a target vector. In experiments on 24 noiseless BBOB functions, 24 reduced-exposure MA-BBOB instances, and 12 newly generated contamination-mitigating test instances, EoTF reliably produces functions with closely matching ELA characteristics and preserves optimizer performance rankings under fixed evaluation budgets, supporting their validity as surrogate benchmarks. While a baseline neural-network-based generator achieves higher accuracy in 2D, EoTF substantially outperforms it in 3D and exhibits stable solution quality as dimensionality increases, highlighting favorable scalability. Compared with the concurrent LLM-based generator LLaMEA, EoTF achieves comparable accuracy, with no statistically significant difference on any of the benchmarks considered. Overall, EoTF offers a practical route to scalable, portable, and interpretable benchmark generation targeted to desired landscape properties.

论文题名

Relation Reasoning with LLMs in Expensive Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3738898 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Expensive optimization problems (EOPs) are black-box tasks with costly objective evaluations and no gradient access, making the evaluation budget the key bottleneck. Surrogate-assisted evolutionary algorithms (SAEAs) reduce evaluations via surrogate predictions, but conventional surrogates often require frequent retraining as populations evolve, incurring overhead. This paper proposes R2SAEA, a reinforcement-trained relation-based large language model (LLM) surrogate-assisted evolutionary algorithm. We cast relation-based surrogate modeling as an in-context pairwise reasoning task. To enable efficient inference in evolutionary loops, we develop an anchor-based iterative context construction strategy that reduces prompt complexity from quadratic to linear in population size for each inference task, and a voting-based aggregation scheme that converts predicted relations into scores for offspring selection. We further build a reinforcement learning pipeline from evolutionary trajectories and fine-tune Qwen2.5 with GRPO. Experiments on single- and multi-objective benchmarks show improved relation prediction and state-of-the-art optimization performance over strong SAEA baselines and general LLMs. Quantization also enables efficient edge deployment, supporting a zero-shot surrogate paradigm without per-generation retraining.

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

A Budget-Aware Feasibility-Guided Surrogate-Assisted Evolutionary Algorithm for Energy-Aware Distributed Hybrid Flow Shop Scheduling

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3737767 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

The energy-aware distributed hybrid flow shop scheduling problem requires coordinated factory assignment, job sequencing, and machine allocation while minimizing total energy consumption (TEC) and makespan. Each true evaluation requires complete schedule decoding, making a fixed number of true schedule evaluations a limited search resource. This paper proposes a Budget-Aware Feasibility-Guided Surrogate-Assisted Evolutionary Algorithm (BF-SAEA) that coordinates surrogate-guided evaluation allocation and subsequent refinement through one global true-evaluation counter. A scale-specific multi-output surrogate and anchor-based trust region guide evaluation allocation, while a dynamically tightened excess-waiting guard provides auxiliary search guidance without changing the biobjective TEC– C max formulation. Budget-controlled local and terminal refinement share the same evaluation resource, and only truly evaluated values are used for population and archive updating and for final performance assessment. Under a common budget of 3000 true evaluations, BF-SAEA significantly outperforms the classical evolutionary baselines and shows competitive performance against the recent scheduling-oriented algorithms.

Kolmogorov-Arnold Classifier Systems as Universal Approximators

Hiroki Shiraishi; Hisao Ishibuchi; Masaya Nakata

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3736664
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Kolmogorov-Arnold Classifier Systems as Universal Approximators

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3736664 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

As the input dimension n grows, rule-based machine learning, such as Learning Classifier Systems (LCSs), faces a fundamental scalability bottleneck for function approximation: both rule count and parameter count grow exponentially with n . Traditional LCSs partition the n -dimensional input space directly, requiring O ( m n ) rules for adequate coverage, where m is the per-variable resolution. This article breaks from this paradigm by reorganizing rules dimension-wise, guided by the Kolmogorov-Arnold representation theorem: any continuous n -dimensional function can be expressed as a finite superposition of one-dimensional functions. The proposed Kolmogorov-Arnold Classifier System (KACS) decomposes the target function into one-dimensional subproblems and assigns a dedicated ruleset to each, reducing the worst-case rule count from O ( m n ) to O ( mn 2 ) and replacing n -dimensional local models with one-dimensional models requiring only two parameters per rule, independent of n. We also provide the first constructive proof that an LCS, namely KACS, is a universal approximator for continuous functions on compact domains. Evaluated against a direct n -dimensional input space partitioning approach under otherwise identical conditions, KACS achieves competitive accuracy in many settings while using only 2% to 40% of the parameters.

TransGP: Task-Conditioned Transformer-Guided Genetic Programming for Multitask Dynamic Flexible Job Shop Scheduling

Meng Xu; Jiao Liu; Hua Yu; Yew Soon Ong

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3734959
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

TransGP: Task-Conditioned Transformer-Guided Genetic Programming for Multitask Dynamic Flexible Job Shop Scheduling

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3734959 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Hyper-heuristics are widely used for dynamic flexible job shop scheduling (DFJSS), where scheduling heuristics must make effective sequencing and routing decisions under uncertain shop-floor conditions. Genetic programming (GP) is a common hyper-heuristic approach because it can evolve symbolic, non-differentiable scheduling rules. However, conventional GP relies mainly on stochastic variation and simulation-based selection, which can lead to slow convergence and inefficient exploration of the heuristic space. Multitask GP can improve search by solving related scheduling tasks jointly, but existing methods mainly transfer heuristic components across tasks and do not explicitly generate task-conditioned rule structures. To address this limitation, we propose TransGP, a task-conditioned Transformer-guided GP framework for multitask heuristic learning in DFJSS. The Transformer is trained from elite symbolic rules and is used as a learned mutation operator during GP evolution. It provides two forms of guidance: it biases mutation toward structural patterns observed in high-quality heuristics, and it conditions rule generation on task information. In this way, TransGP combines the interpretability and simulation-based selection of GP with the generative capability of a task-conditioned Transformer. Experimental results show that TransGP performs strongly against GP, surrogate-assisted GP, DRL-based methods, handcrafted heuristics, and a pure Transformer baseline. Ablation and further analyses confirm that Transformer guidance is most effective when integrated with GP, improving search while preserving behavioural diversity and reusable symbolic patterns across related tasks.

UniCC: A Unified Coevolutionary Architecture with Divergence-Speedup Modeling for Large-Scale Global Optimization

Wenjie Qiu; Zhenrong Weng; Yue-Jiao Gong; Wei-Neng Chen; Jun Zhang
