官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

UniCC: A Unified Coevolutionary Architecture with Divergence-Speedup Modeling for Large-Scale Global Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3733580 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Cooperative coevolution (CC) is a powerful paradigm for large-scale global optimization, mitigating the curse of dimensionality. Existing CC studies mainly focus on problem decomposition and resource allocation, while the coevolutionary architecture, i.e., how decomposed subspaces are coordinated during optimization, remains insufficiently studied. This architectural scheduling layer can critically affect both computational efficiency and solution quality, especially for problems with overlapping subspaces. However, existing designs are largely confined to two canonical extremes, serial and parallel modes, which may struggle with overlapping problems whose separability lies between fully separable and non-separable cases. To bridge this gap, we propose UniCC, a unified coevolutionary architecture that coordinates subspaces after decomposition. UniCC is independent of specific decomposition strategies and suboptimizers, and introduces a parameterized scheduling layer that subsumes conventional architectures while substantially expanding the architectural design space. We further develop a unified analytical model and performance indicators to characterize different architectures and explain their behaviors. Experiments on representative LSGO benchmarks demonstrate that UniCC achieves competitive optimization performance with near-linear speedup. Further analyses reveal the effects of key architectural parameters and suggest a promising direction toward adaptive architecture configuration.

Spike-driven Sparse Binary Representation Learning for Large-scale Multi-objective Optimization

Fei Ming; Xinming Shi; Wenxuan Pan; Yaochu Jin

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3732805
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Spike-driven Sparse Binary Representation Learning for Large-scale Multi-objective Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3732805 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Many real-world multi-objective optimization problems contain binary-encoding and large-scale decision variables but sparse Pareto solutions, posing stiff challenges to evolutionary algorithms. With the rapid developments and broad applications of deep neural networks (DNNs), DNN-based decision space reduction has attracted increasing attention in this area. Nevertheless, the computational overhead of DNNs is a lasting challenge, and existing methods struggle to learn sparse binary representations as their continuous activations and gradient-based updates may inherently mismatch. Differently, as the third generation of artificial neural networks, spiking neural networks (SNNs) offer a more natural and efficient framework for representing sparse binary data owing to their spike-triggered event-driven working mechanism. This paper presents a first attempt to introduce spike-driven sparse binary representation learning into sparse large-scale multi-objective optimization problems (SLMOPs). Specifically, a lightweight SNN with leaky integrate-and-fire neurons is developed for evolutionary online solution modeling in solving SLMOPs. In the training process, we integrate Hebbian learning to learn the patterns of non-zero variables as local plasticity, and Kullback-Leibler divergence loss to learn the distribution of obtained Pareto solutions as global plasticity. Moreover, an adaptive firing threshold mechanism is proposed to improve the versatility across problems with different sparsities and avoid parameter tuning. Experimental results demonstrate the advantage of our methods over algorithms tailored for SLMOPs on benchmark and 20 real-world problems from different areas in terms of both effectiveness and efficiency, especially on real-world problems with unknown characteristics and sparsities.

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

A Fractional-Order Gradient Guided Dynamic Block-Coordinate Evolution Strategy for Large-Scale Expensive Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3731524 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Large-scale expensive optimization problems (LSEOPs) present a formidable challenge characterized by high-dimensional search spaces constrained by strictly limited evaluation budgets. Although surrogate-assisted evolutionary algorithms (SAEAs) have been widely adopted to mitigate evaluation costs, they often grapple with the prohibitive computational overhead of model training and the deterioration of surrogate accuracy as dimensionality increases. To address these limitations, this work proposes a Fractional-Order Block-Coordinate Evolution Strategy (FOBES) for LSEOPs, which departs from the conventional paradigm of fitness prediction. Instead of relying on complex surrogate models, the proposed method uses fractional-order gradient memory to extract and accumulate directional information from historical evaluations. This estimated ideal direction guides a novel directional consistency pre-screening mechanism, enabling efficient identification of promising candidate solutions within a block-coordinate descent framework. Furthermore, a dynamic random grouping strategy and an adaptive data-driven sampling technique are dynamically integrated to facilitate a smooth transition from exploration to exploitation. Experimental results on the CEC’2013 benchmark suite and the extended CEC’2010 suite with up to 5000 dimensions demonstrate that FOBES significantly outperforms state-of-the-art algorithms in terms of both solution quality and computational efficiency.

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Parallel Genetic Algorithm with Mutual Information and Parent Set Structure for Bayesian Network Structure Learning

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3730953 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

The task of Bayesian network structure learning (BNSL) is widely recognized as an NP-hard problem. Genetic algorithms (GA) have shown significant efficacy in addressing combinatorial optimization problems, including those related to BNSL. However, when dealing with large-scale datasets and high-dimensional networks, existing GA-based BNSL methods often encounter extensive time consumption and involve numerous redundant calculations. To address these issues, this paper proposes a full-process parallel GA for BNSL with mutual information and parent set structure (PGA-MIPS), which is driven by a general parallel distributed framework implemented on Apache Spark. A strategy with parent set structure decomposition and memorization is designed to avoid redundant calculations in fitness evaluation. The proposed GA uses customized crossover and mutation operators, based on the parent set structure and mutual information to preserve advantageous substructures and simultaneously increase population diversity. The experimental results on eight Bayesian networks show that PGA-MIPS outperforms the compared algorithms in terms of both consistency and structural Hamming distance with the shortest runtime in most cases.

Derivative-free Evolutionary Search with Stochastic Approximation and Brownian Repulsion

Rajdeep Dutta; T Venkatesh Varma; Saikat Sarkar; Mariya Mamajiwala; Noor Awad; Senthilnath Jayavelu; Debasish Roy

卷期
V · I—
门控

DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3730903
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Derivative-free Evolutionary Search with Stochastic Approximation and Brownian Repulsion

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3730903 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Many global optimization algorithms rely on stochastic search, but often lack a principled probabilistic foundation. We introduce a stochastic approximation and Brownian repulsion based evolutionary search, code-named SABRES, that offers a balance of exploitation and exploration using diffusive stochastic processes. The exploitation step employs a Robbins–Monro type stochastic approximation to provide computationally inexpensive, directionally consistent updates. Exploration is through a change of probability measures. It induces a repulsive drift among trajectories, implemented through a regularized Brownian interaction. This facilitates an efficient coverage of the search space whilst avoiding early collapse to local minima. We establish existence and uniqueness of solutions and prove almost-sure convergence in discrete time. A two-timescale convergence proof is provided in the Supplementary Material. Theoretical results are backed up by numerical experiments, first on CEC-2022 benchmark functions and then on a million-dimensional crack-detection optimization problem. analysis and numerical experiments support SABRES as a practically effective framework for derivative-free global optimization.

Evolutionary Sequential Transfer Optimization via Directly Bridging Landscapes for Heterogeneous Expensive Problems

Wangpeng Huang; Cuie Yang; Jinliang Ding; Xingyi Zhang; Kay Chen Tan

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3731030
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Evolutionary Sequential Transfer Optimization via Directly Bridging Landscapes for Heterogeneous Expensive Problems

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3731030 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Evolutionary sequential transfer optimization (ES-TO) algorithms are suitable for computationally expensive optimization problems, since they can transfer knowledge from solved related tasks to reduce computational costs. However, existing ESTO algorithms face significant challenges in expensive optimization where inconsistent dimensions and features across heterogeneous tasks hinder effective knowledge transfer. The current methods attempt to bridge these spaces via alignment, while such mappings inevitably distort landscape geometry, potentially misguiding the evolutionary search. To this end, we develop a DBL-ESTO approach, which directly bridges landscapes using a newly proposed heterogeneous transfer kernel. Specifically, the proposed kernel is designed to directly address dimension mismatch between tasks by incorporating a Dirac delta function without altering the original data structures. Moreover, to capture the heterogeneous data features of tasks, distinct standard kernels are employed for different tasks. To leverage knowledge from multiple source tasks, the heterogeneous transfer kernel assigns a learnable parametric coefficient to model inter-task correlations, which facilitates an adaptive source task selection approach for identifying high-quality source tasks. To directly transfer landscape knowledge, the proposed kernel is incorporated into the transfer Gaussian process regression (TGPR) to enable cross-task landscape sharing, subsequently constructing a surrogate model by aggregating multiple TGPRs. Experimental results on 24 test problems and one real-world problem verify the effectiveness of DBL-ESTO approach.

ExTrEMO-D: Diffusion Transfer for Expensive Multi-Objective Optimization

Haofeng Wu; Jiao Liu; Yaochu Jin; Yew-Soon Ong

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3730004
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

ExTrEMO-D: Diffusion Transfer for Expensive Multi-Objective Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3730004 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

In transfer optimization of expensive multi-objective optimization (ExMO), effectively reusing structural knowledge from Pareto-optimal solutions of source tasks is crucial for improving sample efficiency. This work proposes Expensive Transfer Evolutionary Multi-Objective Optimizer with Diffusion (ExTrEMO-D) , a generative transfer framework that models the Pareto-set distribution p (x) in the decision space. Compared with forward transfer based on supervised surrogate modeling, ExTrEMO-D captures cross-task similarity in the Pareto-set geometric structure, which provides more informative guidance for optimization. Meanwhile, ExTrEMO-D mitigates the model collapse issue in inverse transfer, which can arise from one-to-many mappings introduced by non-Pareto-optimal samples, by avoiding explicit pairing between preference vectors and solutions. ExTrEMO-D realizes diffusion transfer via two-stage training and targetguided sampling. In terms of the two-stage training process, (i) the optimal transport aligns source non-dominated solutions to the target domain, and (ii) diffusion learns a geometry-consistent prior from the aligned samples. Then, target-guided sampling injects gradients from target multi-objective surrogates into the denoising dynamics, steering generation towards the target Pareto front. Extensive experiments on synthetic benchmarks and three real-world applications spanning materials science, robotics, and machine learning demonstrate that ExTrEMO-D consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines and achieves improved sample efficiency in ExMO.

Learning–Optimization Interactive Framework for Evolutionary Multi-Objective Recommendation

Yuanyuan Ge; Haipeng Yang; Fan Cheng; Likang Wu; Hongke Zhao; Lei Zhang

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3729026
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Learning–Optimization Interactive Framework for Evolutionary Multi-Objective Recommendation

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3729026 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Evolutionary multi-objective optimization (EMO) has demonstrated strong capability for balancing conflicting criteria such as accuracy, diversity and novelty in recommender systems (RSs). However, most existing EMO-based RSs adopt a two-stage learning – then – optimization paradigm, in which representation learning and EMO are executed sequentially and independently. In other words, the later optimization stage is constrained by the established prediction scores from the former learning stage, leading to a performance bottleneck in multi-objective recommendation. To address this limitation, we propose a learning–optimization interactive framework (LOIF) that enables a closed-loop feedback interaction between representation learning and EMO. At the core of LOIF, we design an adaptive Pareto-feedback interaction (APFI) strategy to determine when and how information is exchanged between learning and optimization. Learning-state signals, including loss stagnation and embedding stability, determine when prediction results are passed from learning to optimization. The resulting Pareto-optimal solutions determine how information from EMO is fed back into learning via probabilistic sampling. Building upon APFI, we further propose a semantic-enhanced initialization (SEI) strategy to improve representation quality during the learning stage. We also propose a fast search competitive optimization (FSCO) strategy to accelerate Pareto front convergence during the optimization stage, enabling efficient multi-objective search under frequent learning–optimization interactions. Extensive experiments on real-world datasets demonstrate that LOIF consistently outper-forms traditional two-stage frameworks in accuracy–diversity trade-offs and achieves faster Pareto convergence.

论文题名

A Risk-Aware Dynamic Multi-objective Evolutionary Algorithm for Distributed Flexible Job Shop Scheduling with Machine Deterioration

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3728443 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

With the deepening of globalization and industry 4.0, distributed intelligent scheduling has become pivotal to green and sustainable manufacturing. However, real-world production not only faces performance degradation caused by the continuous deterioration of machines, but also often encounters a high degree of uncertainty in processing time due to various factors. To solve this problem, this paper constructs a dynamic distributed scheduling model integrating machine deterioration and type-2 fuzzy processing times. In order to realize the collaborative optimization of makespan and energy consumption, this paper proposes a risk-aware dynamic multi-objective evolutionary algorithm (RADEA). Firstly, proactive-reactive reliability governance (PR2G) is designed. It establishes a double-loop control system that actively predicts failure probability to trigger proactive maintenance (proactive loop) and quantifies environmental drift to adjust search intensity (reactive loop), thereby intelligently balancing risk mitigation and exploration. In addition, a novel predictive-risk-aware tchebycheff (PR-TCH) aggregation function is designed to quantitatively integrate reliability assessments into the selection process, implicitly filtering out fragile schedules. Experimental results on 30 instances against four advanced algorithms demonstrate that RADEA exhibits superior competitiveness, particularly in proactively mitigating quality risks and maintaining solution diversity in stochastic environments.

A Decoupled Basis-Vector-Driven Generative Framework for Dynamic Multi-Objective Optimization

Yaoming Yang; Shuai Wang; Bingdong Li; Peng Yang; Ke Tang

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

A Decoupled Basis-Vector-Driven Generative Framework for Dynamic Multi-Objective Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3727397 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Dynamic multi-objective optimization problems require algorithms to track the time-varying Pareto set and Pareto front as environments change. This is particularly difficult under irregular changes and sparse observations, which weaken short-term prediction, increase the risk of negative transfer, and aggravate the cold-start problem in online adaptation. We therefore propose DB-GEN, a decoupled basis-vector-driven generative framework for online population reinitialization. DB-GEN uses frequency decoupling based on the discrete wavelet transform to represent evolutionary trajectories across multiple scales and sparse dictionary learning to extract transferable basis vectors. A topology-aware contrastive loss then guides their recombination into a structured latent manifold, from which surrogate-assisted generative search constructs initial populations for new environments. Pre-trained offline on 120 million solutions, DB-GEN can directly handle unseen target problems whose dynamics can be represented by learned basis combinations, without target-specific retraining or fine-tuning. Across 76 configurations, DB-GEN achieves the best MIGD in 57 cases and the second-best in nine.

Jiangyu Wang; Ding Wang; Derong Liu; Junfei Qiao

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Efficient Exploration With Diversity Guarantee Towards Evolutionary Critic Designs

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3727302 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Online learning is widely used for policy optimization in reinforcement learning. However, traditional online learning methods commonly suffer from inefficient exploration in high-dimensional spaces, skewed data distributions, and catastrophic forgetting. To address these limitations, evolutionary critic designs are developed by integrating evolutionary strategies with an explicit diversity-preserving mechanism for policy exploration. A population of policies is maintained and evolved within the framework. Each policy is evaluated using a composite fitness function, in which cost minimization is balanced against a diversity penalty. The best policies are retained across generations through elite preservation. The dual-buffer experience replay is used to mitigate catastrophic forgetting and improve data utilization for policy training. Simulations on a robotic manipulator and a Quanser helicopter are conducted to evaluate the proposed framework. The results indicate improvements in learning reliability, exploration efficiency, and state-distribution uniformity.

Adaptive Cascaded Selection via Tensor-Accelerated Unbounded Archive for Multimodal Multi-objective Optimization

Zhiyang Zhang; Qianlong Dang; Baosheng Li; Tingting Wang

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Adaptive Cascaded Selection via Tensor-Accelerated Unbounded Archive for Multimodal Multi-objective Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3727262 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Solving multimodal multi-objective optimization problems requires simultaneously locating multiple global and local Pareto optimal sets (PSs) in the decision space. However, most existing multimodal multi-objective evolutionary algorithms (MMOEAs) are limited by fixed-size populations and aggressive truncation strategies, which often lead to the premature elimination of potentially valuable solutions. To address this issue, an unbounded archive-based evolutionary algorithm (UAEA) is proposed. It utilizes a fully preserved unbounded archive as the evolutionary unit, and maximizes the retention of evolutionary knowledge to guide the search direction. To overcome the computational and storage challenges introduced by the unbounded archive, a block-wise tensor computation paradigm is proposed, and the efficiency is validated through stress tests. This paradigm decomposes large-scale tensor operations into a series of memory-safe subtasks to reduce the space complexity and utilize the parallel computing advantages of tensors. Based on this, an adaptive cascaded parent selection mechanism is designed, which selects parents directly from the archive through a cascade of neighborhood dominance intensity, dynamic convergence filtering, and roulette selection. Furthermore, a landscape feature detector is constructed to perceive the existence of local PSs, thereby dynamically regulating the convergence pressure to obtain global and local PSs. Comparison experiments on five benchmark test suites and two real-world problems demonstrate that UAEA achieves better performance than seven competitive MMOEAs.

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

A Knowledge-enhanced Framework with k-step DQN for No-Wait Flow-Shop Scheduling with Sequence-Dependent Setup Time

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3727219 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

The no-wait flow shop scheduling problem with sequence-dependent setup time (NWFSSP-SDST) is an important scenario in the discrete manufacturing industry. The NWFSSP-SDST with the objective of the maximum completion time (makespan) is investigated in this paper. A knowledge-enhanced co-evolution framework with k -step deep Q-network mechanism (KECE-KDQN) with four phases is proposed to address the NWFSSP-SDST. Task similarity (TS) and structural similarity (SS) are utilized to calculate the distance of individuals, which is used to select candidates during the population exploration phase. In the individual explanation phase, a policy self-selection mechanism is proposed to select an individual after neigh-borhood generation via k -step deep Q-network ( k -step DQN). In the knowledge enhancement phase, knowledge nodes are extracted from the elite individual and dataset. These nodes are embedded into the population to facilitate the learning of high-quality knowledge. Three properties of the effectiveness of knowledge node embedding are proved mathematically. A hybrid tournament selection algorithm (HTSA) is proposed to recombine populations and generate offspring in the population recombination phase. Extensive experiments on base, large-scale, benchmark, and small-scale validation instances validate the effectiveness of the proposed strategies by KECE-KDQN. A comparison with the state-of-the-art algorithms demonstrates the search effectiveness and strong solution quality of the KECE-KDQN in solving the NWFSSP-SDST.

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Evolving Jailbreaks: Automated Multi-Objective Long-Tail Attacks on Large Language Models

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3726531 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in Web-facing applications, raising concerns about jailbreak attacks that bypass ethical and safety constraints. While prior work has demonstrated the effectiveness of automated jailbreaks, most methods operate within conventional natural language distributions, limiting their stealthiness and structural diversity. This paper focuses on long-tail jailbreak attacks, which exploit rare or structured input patterns, such as code-oriented or encryption-style prompts, to evade safety alignment mechanisms. To systematically explore this complex search space, an LLM-assisted evolutionary framework, EvoJail, is proposed. Each candidate prompt is represented as a semantic-algorithmic individual that integrates natural language descriptions with a soft reversible encode-decode mechanism. EvoJail leverages LLMs for initialization, mutation, crossover, and repair of candidates, while a multi-objective evolutionary process jointly optimizes attack success rate and output fluency. Extensive experiments across multiple target models, benchmark datasets, and evaluation metrics demonstrate the competitiveness of EvoJail against existing methods. These results highlight the potential of combining LLM reasoning with evolutionary search for automated discovery of robust long-tail jailbreak prompts.

A Co-evolutionary Algorithm with Multiple Local Search Strategies for Solving the Electric Vehicle Routing Problem

Yukang Su; Shuo Zhang; Yiquan Wang; Yu Yang; Qiran Zhao

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

A Co-evolutionary Algorithm with Multiple Local Search Strategies for Solving the Electric Vehicle Routing Problem

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3725683 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

With the popularization of electric vehicles, the Electric Vehicle Routing Problem has become a research hotspot in combinatorial optimization. The addition of battery capacity constraints increases the problem complexity, which can be decomposed into two optimization components—customer routing and charging strategy—for collaborative optimization. This paper proposes a co-evolutionary algorithm with multiple local search strategies to solve this problem. First, ant colony optimization is used for initial solution generation to ensure population diversity. Subsequently, an adaptive genetic algorithm considering 4 crossover operators and 3 mutation operators is designed to optimize customer routing schemes, followed by the use of a binary genetic algorithm to optimize the insertion positions of depots and charging stations. Meanwhile, during the co-evolutionary process, route repair attempts are performed on infeasible solutions to expand the search space. Finally, two local search algorithms—stepwise greedy search and large neighborhood search—are designed to conduct neighborhood searches on promising solutions. Experiments based on the R-C datasets and the E-X datasets verify that the proposed algorithm achieves breakthroughs in the optimal results on 19 instances and significantly outperforms previous co-evolutionary algorithms in terms of operational efficiency.

DE-PCGrad Evol: Gradient Conflict Resolution for Diffusion Constrained Multi-Objective 3D Molecular Generation

Meijia Song; Heming Jia; Shuyuan Luo; Juan Liu; Xiangrong Liu

论文题名

DE-PCGrad Evol: Gradient Conflict Resolution for Diffusion Constrained Multi-Objective 3D Molecular Generation

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3726116 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

The discovery of novel and structurally diverse molecular candidates is pivotal to drug development and materials science. Despite remarkable advances in diffusion-based generative methods, current techniques still suffer from low efficiency and poor flexibility in generating 3D molecules that satisfy multiple property requirements under strict structural constraints. To address these limitations, this work proposes DE-PCGrad Evol, a training-free, target-oriented framework built upon Differential Evolution (DE) for constrained multi-objective 3D molecular generation. Its core innovation is a DE-based gradient conflict resolution mechanism, which mutates multi-objective gradients to preserve the high-quality features of each objective in a directional manner. Additionally, we analyze selection strategies, introduce a novel composition-level evaluation metric, Atom Uniqueness, which complements existing molecular assessment systems, and propose two strategies to enhance search space exploration. Experimental results show DE-PCGrad Evol leverages DE’s strengths to achieve superior performance in dual- and tri-objective tasks with atomic/molecular stability constraints. Notably, it can generate valid molecules even when starting from initially invalid structures. These findings highlight its practical potential to accelerate drug discovery and advance materials design.

Weiqi Liu; Shaolong Shi; Jiawei Zou; Qiujun Huang; Jinting Wang

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Fitness-Dependent Hierarchical Mutation for Nanorobot Morphology–Control Co-Evolution in Biological Gradient Field

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3725855 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

In evolutionary robotics, applying uniform variation operators across all individuals can disrupt well-adapted morphologies while providing insufficient exploration for low-fitness solutions, especially acute in dynamic, biologically structured environments. Inspired by the biological phenomenon of fitness-dependent hypermutation, we propose a Hierarchical Operator Selection mechanism integrated with Age-Fitness Pareto Optimization (HOS-AFPO) for the co-evolution of nanorobot morphology and control within biological gradient field (BGF). HOS partitions the population into three fitness strata and assigns distinct variation strategies: an LLM-guided conservative mutation for elite individuals to preserve valuable adaptations; random exploration for the middle layer to maintain diversity; and a PPO-driven hypermutation engine for low-fitness individuals to aggressively escape local optima. The framework is evaluated under a COMSOL-derived tumor microenvironment model on locomotion and tumor-targeting tasks. Experimental results demonstrate that HOS-AFPO delivers superior best-fitness performance over representative operator-selection baselines, together with competitive hypervolume and compact, task-adaptive morphologies. Ablation studies support the synergistic contributions of hierarchical stratification, LLM-based elite protection, and PPO-based adaptive hypermutation, validating the framework’s effectiveness for embodied intelligence in complex dynamic environments.

Dimensionality Reduction Using Variational Autoencoders in Constrained Evolutionary Multiobjective Optimization

Yanhui Tang; Hui Li; Yuting Bai; Jianyong Sun

卷期

门控
NEW_ARTICLE

10.1109/tevc.2026.3725542
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Dimensionality Reduction Using Variational Autoencoders in Constrained Evolutionary Multiobjective Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3725542 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Constrained multi-objective optimization problems (CMOPs) involving linear equality constraints arise in many real applications. Linear equality constraints confine the feasible region to a low-dimensional manifold embedded in the original decision space, which poses significant difficulties for conventional constraint-handling mechanisms in evolutionary algorithms (EAs). To overcome this challenge, we propose a variational autoencoder (VAE)-assisted evolutionary framework, termed L-MOEA-VAE. The VAE learns a low-dimensional latent representation of the feasible region from the nondominated solutions collected during the evolutionary process. Offspring are generated in both the learned latent space and the original decision space, with a probability parameter that adaptively allocates the search effort between the two spaces. This probability is updated online based on the survival rate of VAE-generated offspring. Additionally, a projection-based repair operator is employed to map infeasible solutions back onto the feasible manifold. Experimental results on a set of benchmark multiobjective test problems with linear equality constraints demonstrate that the proposed framework achieves superior performance in terms of both convergence and diversity compared to several existing baseline or recent constrained multi-objective evolutionary algorithms.

A Transformer-Based Spatiotemporal Perception Framework for Dynamic Constrained Multi-objective Optimization

Chongshuang Hu; Rui Wang; Yukun Du; Minghao Li; Jiang Jiang

A Transformer-Based Spatiotemporal Perception Framework for Dynamic Constrained Multi-objective Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3723845 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Dynamic Constrained Multi-Objective Optimization Problems (DCMOPs) are widely encountered in real-world engineering applications, where both objective functions and constraints change over time, significantly increasing the complexity of problem-solving. Existing approaches still face limitations in historical information utilization, prediction accuracy of feasible solutions, and maintenance of population diversity—particularly in environment involving rapid and drastic shifts in feasible regions. In response to these challenges, this paper proposes a Transformer-Based Spatiotemporal Perception Framework (TSPF). By exploiting the Transformer’s strength in long-sequence modeling, the framework learns temporal and spatial dependencies from historical population to forecast future feasible and high-value regions. Furthermore, a memory-restart mechanism based on environmental similarity and a clustering-guided selection strategy are introduced to enhance the stability and diversity of the population. Experimental results on 18 benchmark problems with varying severities and frequencies of change and a real-world case study indicate that the proposed method achieves superior performance compared to several state-of-the-art algorithms, demonstrating its effectiveness and robustness in solving complex dynamic optimization problems.

Two Models with K-means and DQN Assisted Meta-heuristics for Scheduling Multi-objective Amphibious Unmanned Surface Vehicles

Hui Yu; Kaizhou Gao; Ponnuthurai Nagaratnam Suganthan; Peiyong Duan; Zhi-Hui Zhan

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Two Models with K-means and DQN Assisted Meta-heuristics for Scheduling Multi-objective Amphibious Unmanned Surface Vehicles

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3722917 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

With the development of unmanned surface vehicle (USV) and amphibious USV (AUSV) technologies, the corresponding coordination and scheduling problems are receiving increasing attention. However, the existing studies predominantly focus on the scheduling of USVs, with limited attention to the AUSV scheduling problems. This work considers the multi-objective AUSV scheduling problems with obstacle avoidance. The objectives are to minimize the maximum completion time and total energy consumption, simultaneously. First, two mathematical models are established to describe the concerned problems. Second, an improved A* algorithm is employed for avoiding obstacles. Third, four well-known meta-heuristics (artificial bee colony, genetic algorithm, particle swarm optimization, and Jaya algorithm) are employed and improved to solve the AUSV scheduling problems. Three K-means strategies are constructed to boost the quality of initial solutions. Seven problem-feature-based local search operators are designed to enhance the search efficiency of meta-heuristics. Then, a deep Q-network with problem-specific state representations and objective-based reward functions is designed to intelligently recommend suitable local search operators during iterations, thereby accelerating convergence. Finally, two mathematical models are solved by the Gurobi solver. The effectiveness of the improved strategies and the competitiveness of proposed algorithms are rigorously evaluated across 21 instances with different scales. Among all the compared algorithms, the enhanced Jaya algorithm with deep Q-network demonstrates the best overall performance.

Mixture of Experts Learning-based Accurate Surrogate for Evolutionary Expensive Optimization

Chuanbin Liu; Nankun Mu; Jian-Yu Li

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3723213
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Mixture of Experts Learning-based Accurate Surrogate for Evolutionary Expensive Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3723213 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Evolutionary expensive optimization aims to solve optimization problems with expensive fitness evaluations, where data-driven evolutionary algorithms (DDEAs) learn fitness knowledge from evaluated data for constructing surrogates to approximate the fitness landscape and aid evolutionary search. However, different evolutionary stages may need different fitness knowledge for constructing a suitable surrogate, making it difficult for existing DDEAs to provide consistently accurate fitness approximation. Therefore, how to automatically learn the most suitable fitness knowledge for different search states remains a key challenge in DDEAs. To address this, this article proposes a mixture of experts (MoE) learning-based DDEA, termed MoE-DDEA. Different from existing DDEAs, the MoE-DDEA automatically learns the most suitable fitness knowledge across different search regions and evolutionary stages to guide optimization, along with three novel designs. First, a MoE-based learning framework is proposed, which builds expert models that learn different fitness knowledge and a gating model for expert activation. Second, an activation strategy is proposed to activate suitable expert models via the gating model for constructing surrogates, thereby leveraging the relevant fitness knowledge within the activated expert models across different evolutionary stages. Third, a knowledge-based initialization strategy is proposed to leverage prior knowledge from evaluated data to alleviate the cold-start problem and enhance search efficiency. The efficiency of the MoE-DDEA is validated not only on popular test suites but also on a practical application of the evaluation expert assignment problem for project evaluation. These suggest that MoE-DDEA is a promising and competitive learning approach for challenging expensive optimization.

论文题名

Branching Deep Q-Network Enhanced Memetic Algorithm for Skilled-Worker Constrained Flexible Job Shop Scheduling Problem

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3722402 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

The flexible job shop scheduling problem (FJSP) traditionally emphasizes machine flexibility, while worker heterogeneity is often simplified or neglected. In practical manufacturing systems, worker skill levels affect both processing efficiency and task feasibility, leading to the skilled-worker constrained flexible job shop scheduling problem (SWC-FJSP). This article investigates the SWC-FJSP with multi-level worker skills and skill-dependent processing efficiency, aiming to minimize the makespan. A mixed-integer linear programming model is first formulated to characterize the coupled operation–machine–worker assignment decisions. Then, a branching deep Q-network enhanced memetic algorithm, termed MABDQN, is developed as a learning-assisted evolutionary framework. MABDQN integrates an adaptive hybrid initialization strategy to generate high-quality and diverse populations, a BDQN-driven operator orchestration mechanism to select operator category and execution intensity according to the evolving search state, and a multi-stage adaptive restart strategy guided by a stage adaptive reward function to alleviate premature convergence. Extensive experiments on a newly constructed test set with different instance scales show that MABDQN outperforms several state-of-the-art algorithms in solution quality and robustness. Ablation studies further verify the contribution of the main components and demonstrate the effectiveness of state conditioned operator orchestration for complex SWC-FJSP instances.

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Bio-Inspired Neural Network for Energy-Aware Complete Coverage Path Planning of Reconfigurable Robot

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3721909 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Reconfigurable robots can dynamically adapt their footprint, enabling efficient traversal of narrow passages and more productive coverage in open spaces. Such adaptability is crucial for complete coverage path planning (CCPP), where both spatial constraints and energy efficiency must be addressed simultaneously. This paper presents an energy-aware CCPP framework for footprint-adaptive reconfigurable robots based on an extended n -state Glasius Bio-inspired Neural Network, denoted EGBNN (n) . The proposed method combines biological neural dynamics with an energy-aware fitness-based decision mechanism to evaluate candidate motion and morphology transitions during online planning. An energy-aware cost function, embedded directly into the neural decision process, jointly models the robot’s transformation, translation, and rotational actions, allowing the robot to selectively compress in constrained regions and expand to maximize coverage efficiency. Extending from the classical population-based evolutionary algorithms, the proposed framework performs sequential online selection over candidate neighboring cell–morphology pairs using a fitness-like objective that balances spatial desirability and energy cost. Simulation studies and real-world experiments with a reconfigurable robot demonstrate that EGBNN (n) consistently reduces energy consumption compared to state-of-the-art methods, achieving up to a 31.1% reduction while maintaining complete coverage.

Illuminating Heuristic Design: Language-Model-Driven Quality-Diversity Evolution for Combinatorial Optimization

Shikun Chen; Yangguang Liu

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Illuminating Heuristic Design: Language-Model-Driven Quality-Diversity Evolution for Combinatorial Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3721299 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Current methods that use large language models to evolve heuristics for combinatorial optimization converge toward a single best algorithm or a small set of trade-off solutions. When the problem distribution shifts, these methods require expensive re-adaptation and their performance degrades. Here, we propose a quality-diversity evolutionary framework that maintains a structured archive of diverse heuristics, where each is specialized for a particular class of problem instances. A large language model serves as the variation operator within the evolutionary loop. Shapley additive explanations on abstract syntax tree features play a dual role in this framework: they guide the mutation prompts toward productive code modifications, and they extract the behavioral descriptors that organize the archive. An online selector then retrieves the best-matched heuristic for each new unseen instance. We evaluate the approach on vehicle routing, traveling salesman, and job shop scheduling problems under static, perturbed, stochastic, and dynamic conditions. The archive coverage provides inherent robustness to distributional shift, whereas single-heuristic baselines and neural solvers degrade considerably. The resulting heuristic library is interpretable, since each archive cell contains readable code at known feature coordinates.

Evolving Similarity Functions for Graph-based Semi-Supervised Learning

Márcio P. Basgalupp; Ferrante Neri; Rafael G. Rossi; Rodrigo C. Barros; Ricardo Cerri; Teresa B. Ludermir

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Evolving Similarity Functions for Graph-based Semi-Supervised Learning

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3717536 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Automatic data classification is commonly performed using supervised learning algorithms that build models to classify new instances. However, labelled data are often scarce and expensive to obtain. Semi-supervised learning (SSL) offers a viable alternative by exploiting a few labelled and many unlabelled instances. Among various SSL approaches, graph-based methods are particularly appealing as they rely on neighbourhood structure rather than explicit parametric distributional assumptions. Nevertheless, their performance heavily depends on graph construction, which requires defining a suitable similarity (or distance) function. Since no single similarity function performs optimally across all datasets, this paper introduces two evolutionary approaches – GEAS and ST-GPAS – to automatically design similarity functions for graph-based SSL. Extensive experiments on 50 public datasets show that the proposed methods yield similarity functions leading to superior transductive learning performance compared with traditional distance-based measures and representative metric-learning algorithms. The improvements, measured in Micro- F 1 and Macro- F 1 , are statistically significant under nonparametric tests. GEAS and ST-GPAS achieve closely matched performance, with small but consistent advantages depending on the supervision regime.

Multiple-Gain Model to Estimate Runtime of Evolutionary Algorithms for Constrained Discrete Optimization

Tongli He; Han Huang; Pengxiang Chen; Fangqing Liu; Chunguo Wu; Zhifeng Hao

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Multiple-Gain Model to Estimate Runtime of Evolutionary Algorithms for Constrained Discrete Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3715876 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Evolutionary algorithms (EAs) have been extensively applied to constrained discrete optimization problems. However, their theoretical runtime analysis for practical instances remains many open problems. While the average gain model has advanced runtime analysis for continuous EAs, we demonstrate that its direct application to discrete problems with non-smooth gain trends may produce inaccurate runtime estimates. To address this challenge, we propose a multiple-gain model. This model yields runtime bounds based on the gains over multiple iterations, rather than relying on a single step. We integrate sampling and data fitting techniques to simulate these multiple gains and construct the desired runtime estimation function. These techniques enable us to estimate the runtime for EAs solving practical constrained discrete problems. Experiments validate the estimated runtime against existing theoretical bounds on pseudo-Boolean functions and the chance-constrained knapsack problem. Furthermore, we apply our method to estimate runtime for the multi-dimensional knapsack problem, an open issue in evolutionary computation theory. Comparative numerical results demonstrate the model applicability to practical problems, confirming that the estimated runtimes are closely aligned with empirical observations.

Multitree Genetic Programming for Multimodal Feature Construction in Skin Cancer Diagnosis

Zhicheng Wu; Bing Xue; Mengjie Zhang

论文题名

Multitree Genetic Programming for Multimodal Feature Construction in Skin Cancer Diagnosis

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3715688 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Early and accurate diagnosis of skin cancer, particularly melanoma (MEL), is critical for patient survival. Traditional computer-aided diagnosis (CAD) systems often require extensive expert knowledge, and it is difficult to provide a comprehensive representation of skin lesions, while convolutional neural network–based CAD systems are less practical when data are limited due to medical privacy constraints. With the increasing availability of multimodal data, there is a rising demand for developing multimodal models. Tree-based genetic programming, especially multitree GP, has shown promising potential in feature construction and multimodal learning. However, current multitree GP methods remain limited in multimodal skin cancer diagnosis. This paper proposes a multitree genetic programming approach to skin cancer diagnosis (MTGPSCD) that enables automatic feature construction from multimodal data. Specifically, a new multitree representation is designed, consisting of modality-specific trees (color, shape, texture, and patient metadata) and two fusion trees that integrate multimodal information at both low and high levels. Moreover, a multimodal hybrid fusion (MHF) method is devised to sequentially refine image-based decisions with metadata cues, mimicking clinical diagnostic reasoning. To enhance search ability, an offspring generation strategy (OGS) dynamically adjusts crossover and mutation rates and evolves subpopulations independently to improve population diversity. Extensive experiments on six multimodal skin cancer datasets demonstrate that MTGPSCD significantly outperforms single-modality and most existing multimodal approaches. Further analyses confirm that MTGPSCD can automatically evolve compact and effective multitree GP models for feature construction and fusion from multimodal data. The evolved individuals can capture discriminative characteristics for distinguishing different skin cancer classes.

Integrating Analytical Physics with LLM-Guided Reward Evolution in Reinforcement Learning

Mingyang Sun; Pengxiang Ding; Donglin Wang; Jianhua Sun; Hua Yu

证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Integrating Analytical Physics with LLM-Guided Reward Evolution in Reinforcement Learning

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3714198 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Reinforcement learning (RL) has become central to enabling robots to act in complex real-world environments, but its success relies heavily on well-designed reward functions. Recent approaches employ large language models (LLMs) to automatically generate and evolve rewards, such as Eureka. While effective in capturing semantic intent, LLM-only methods lack physical grounding and often produce rewards that fail to encode physical executability and dynamical constraints for real-world execution. To address this challenge, we introduce Physically Grounded Reward Evolution (PGRE), which integrates analytical physics concepts into the LLM-driven reward evolution loop. PGRE employs LLM to generate reward candidates, and analytic concepts act as a physical surrogate evaluator to filter infeasible designs and provide feedback for refinement. Specifically, PGRE integrates analytic concepts—mathematically parameterized representations of physical common sense—into an evolutionary reward optimization loop. Within this loop, an LLM generates candidate reward functions from task descriptions, while an automated physical evaluator scores each candidate’s feasibility using structural and affordance knowledge extracted from scene observations. This evaluator filters physically implausible rewards before costly RL training and provides quantitative feedback to steer LLM-driven refinement. Experiments on 12 challenging robotic manipulation tasks show that PGRE substantially outperforms existing LLM-based methods and even surpasses human-designed oracle rewards in some cases. These results highlight the necessity of physical grounding for scalable and robust robot learning.

Matrix-Based Role Adaptive Particle Swarm Optimization for Large-Scale Optimization

Qi-Jia Jiang; Jun Zhang; Jing Xu; Zhi-Hui Zhan

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE

OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Matrix-Based Role Adaptive Particle Swarm Optimization for Large-Scale Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3714593 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Evolutionary computation approaches often face two major challenges in large-scale global optimization (LSGO): 1) the difficulty of balancing exploration and exploitation 2) the heavy computational cost induced by high dimensionality. Therefore, this article proposes a novel matrix-based role adaptive particle swarm optimization (MRPSO) for LSGO. Designed to address the twin challenges of balancing exploration-exploitation and managing high computational cost, MRPSO introduces three key innovations within an efficient matrix-based evolutionary framework. First, an adaptive role allocation mechanism dynamically divides the swarm into two specialized groups: an explorer group tasked with maintaining population diversity, and an exploiter group dedicated to accelerating convergence. Second, a particle convergence indicator (PCI) facilitates communication between these groups, signaling when exploration should be intensified. Third, new update processes are defined as exploiters learn from superior explorers while explorers initiate self-reconstruction into unexplored regions when PCI indicates diminished optimization potential. Extensive experiments conducted on the CEC’2013 LSGO benchmark and real-world feature selection applications demonstrate that MRPSO outperforms eight state-of-the-art algorithms. Furthermore, the theoretical convergence of the proposed algorithm is analyzed, and the runtime analysis confirms its significantly superior computational efficiency, particularly in large-scale optimization scenarios.

DS-PSO: A Distance-Aware and Space-Mapped Particle Swarm Optimization Algorithm

Tianzheng Wang; Jian Tang; Shengxiang Yang; Junfei Qiao

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

DS-PSO: A Distance-Aware and Space-Mapped Particle Swarm Optimization Algorithm

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3713919 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Although extensive research has yielded numerous effective particle swarm optimization (PSO) variants through parameter tuning and structural modifications, these approaches frequently exhibit complex structures, reliance on prior knowledge, and limited adaptability. To address these limitations, a distance-aware and space-mapped PSO (DS-PSO) algorithm is proposed. The DS-PSO maintains structural simplicity while enhancing performance through improved search guidance and population diversity. Specifically, a distance-aware mechanism dynamically evaluates particle states based on their distances to the global best-found position obtained so far, adaptively tuning three control parameters to enhance local exploitation. Concurrently, a space-mapped mechanism introduces dimensional mutation to selected particles, thereby improving population diversity and avoiding premature convergence. Comprehensive experimental evaluations on the classical benchmark suite and the CEC 2022 Single-Objective Bound-Constrained Optimization (SO-BO) benchmark suite demonstrate that DS-PSO achieves better best-found solution quality and more stable performance. A practical engineering case is also employed to validate the effectiveness of the DS-PSO.

Microscale Searching-Based Dynamic Constrained Multi-Objective Optimization for Uncrewed Aerial Vehicle Path Planning in Unknown Environments

Qiqi Xie; Wenhua Qiu; Chaoda Peng; Qiong Huang; Peixi Zhuang; Han Huang

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3713137
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Microscale Searching-Based Dynamic Constrained Multi-Objective Optimization for Uncrewed Aerial Vehicle Path Planning in Unknown Environments

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3713137 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Uncrewed aerial vehicle (UAV) path planning in unknown environments involves generating optimal collision-free paths through iterative perception-planning cycles executed concurrently with flight, during which environmental information is incrementally acquired. Recent research has treated UAV path planning as constrained multi-objective optimization problems and applied evolutionary algorithms as solution methods due to their global search ability. However, these approaches rely on prior environmental information and sufficient optimization time, limiting their applicability to real-time unknown environment scenarios. We model UAV path planning in unknown environments as a dynamic constrained multi-objective optimization problem (DCMOP), where each time step corresponds to one perception-planning cycle. Such modeling enables dynamic constrained multi-objective evolutionary algorithms (DCMOEAs) to rapidly adapt to the evolving objectives and constraints induced by incremental sensing. Nevertheless, multiple kinematic and obstacle-avoidance constraints lead to a highly constrained search space, hindering efficient exploration during optimization. To address this challenge, we propose a DCMOEA based on a constraint-guided microscale-searching mechanism. The mechanism restricts the evolutionary search to a valid flight subspace defined by UAV-specific constraints, thereby improving search performance for real-time path planning. Experimental results on six benchmarks across three urban environments validate the advantage of the DCMOP formulation over single-objective alternatives, and demonstrate the superior performance of the proposed algorithm regarding algorithmic stability and solution quality. The proposed algorithm achieves 100% success rates on all benchmarks against five DCMOEA baselines, with an average 52% improvement in mean hypervolume over the best baseline. This study extends evolutionary computation to real-time UAV autonomous navigation in unknown environments.

SDNAS: Neural Architecture Search for Self-Supervised Single-Image Denoising Using Genetic Algorithm with Modular Encoding

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

SDNAS: Neural Architecture Search for Self-Supervised Single-Image Denoising Using Genetic Algorithm with Modular Encoding

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3709472 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Neural Architecture Search (NAS) facilitates the automated design of high-performance network architectures for single-image denoising, significantly reducing the manual effort required by researchers. However, most existing NAS approaches for single-image denoising rely on time-consuming reinforcement learning (RL) strategies, which limits their ability to effectively search for optimal encoder designs and multi-scale structures. Consequently, the resulting architectures often struggle to effectively extract and fuse features across different scales. To address these issues, a self-supervised image denoising NAS (SDNAS) based on Genetic Algorithm (GA) is proposed in this paper. The proposed SDNAS introduces a multi-scale encoder search space, enabling simultaneous optimization of the encoder, decoder, and its multi-scale structure to discover high-performance network architectures. To efficiently represent architectures, a modular encoding strategy is proposed, representing the encoder and decoder structures as parameterized modules. The modular encoding strategy allows for an effective construction of network architectures with compact encoding lengths. Furthermore, a customized GA tailored to the modular encoding strategy is introduced. The algorithm incorporates coarse-grained population initialization, distance-guided parent selection, and module-based crossover and mutation operators to improve search efficiency and accelerate the discovery of optimal architectures. Experimental results show that SDNAS achieves competitive denoising performance with respect to advanced image denoising methods and other NAS methods, validated on both color and grayscale image datasets. The codes and datasets used in this work are available from Google Drive.

Genetic Inspired Embedding Transfer across Independent Knowledge Graphs

Qian Li; Yiming Wang; Yuntao Du; Lizhen Cui

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3708973
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Genetic Inspired Embedding Transfer across Independent Knowledge Graphs

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3708973 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Embedding transfer across Knowledge Graphs (KGs) exhibits diverse types, e.g., the information transferred from source KGs to target KGs is the learned embeddings in the transductive type where entities and relations of targets KGs are seen during training the source KGs, while the relational patterns in the inductive type where target KGs induce new entities under the relational patterns of source KGs. However, existing methods typically assume explicit connections between source and target KGs, limiting their applicability to independent KGs where no prior relational information is available. In this paper, we propose GAKG, a genetic-inspired framework that treats each embedding model as an individual composed of genes and formulates embedding transfer across independent KGs as an evolutionary process in the parameter space. Specifically, for a novel target KG, GAKG initializes the population with pretrained embeddings of existing source KGs and iteratively evolves them through parameter-level crossover, mutation and selection. The evolutionary process is guided by the validation set of the target KG, which is substantially smaller than the training set, thereby reducing computational cost. Extensive experiments on 27 datasets demonstrate that GAKG effectively transfers implicit structural regularities across independent KGs in a lightweight and generalizable manner.

Fast Multi-objective Evolutionary Algorithms to Approximate the Set of Optimal and Nearly Optimal Solutions

Carlos Segura; Angel E. Rodriguez-Fernandez; Carlos Hernández; Oliver Schütze

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3707993
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Fast Multi-objective Evolutionary Algorithms to Approximate the Set of Optimal and Nearly Optimal Solutions

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3707993 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Recently, it has been argued that all nearly optimal solutions of a given multi-objective optimization problem (MOP) are of potential interest for the decision maker (DM). This set, N Q ,ϵ , contains the entire Pareto set (and is thus interesting in the context of multimodal multi-objective optimization) as well as non- but nearly optimal solutions that can still serve as alternative or backup realizations. The DM is hence given an enlarged set of relevant possibilities. In this work, we propose the first evolutionary algorithms aiming for finite-size approximations of the set of nearly optimal solutions of a given MOP. More precisely, we propose the two-phase framework TPEA- N Q ,ϵ . In the first phase, an existing multi-objective evolutionary algorithm (MOEA) can be chosen to obtain a Pareto set approximation, while the second phase focuses on the detection of non- but nearly optimal solutions, with the novelty of using a clustering-based replacement strategy. Efficient algorithms to detect nearly optimal solutions of a discrete set for bi- and three-objective problems are proposed by combining specialized data structures and the two-pointer technique. A methodology to compare methods aiming to approximate N Q ,ϵ is defined. The strength of TPEA- N Q ,ϵ is demonstrated via empirical validation on several benchmark test problems.

LLM-Enhanced Accumulated Learning Prediction for Evolutionary Dynamic Multiobjective Optimization

论文题名

LLM-Enhanced Accumulated Learning Prediction for Evolutionary Dynamic Multiobjective Optimization

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3708317 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

PPrediction-based dynamic multiobjective evolutionary algorithms (DMOEAs) often fail to fully exploit comprehensive historical information when solving dynamic multiobjective optimization problems (DMOPs). To alleviate this challenge, this paper proposes a DMOEA with a large language model (LLM)-enhanced accumulated learning prediction for addressing DMOPs. Specifically, a bidirectional long short-term memory (BiLSTM) network is trained online using approximated Paretooptimal solutions from all previously encountered environments, enabling it to effectively capture the problem’s dynamics and maintain a favorable trade-off between convergence and diversity. Moreover, as a novel contribution to this field, an LLM is used to refine the BiLSTM model by generating high-quality solutions as enhanced training data, thereby substantially reducing prediction error and enhancing robustness. Comprehensive empirical studies have been conducted on 14 commonly used benchmark DMOPs and a real-world problem, which demonstrate the superior performance of the proposed algorithm over five competitive DMOEAs.

Learning Where, What and How to Transfer: A Multi-Role Reinforcement Learning Approach for Evolutionary Multitasking

Jiajun Zhan; Zeyuan Ma; Yue-Jiao Gong; Kay Chen Tan

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Learning Where, What and How to Transfer: A Multi-Role Reinforcement Learning Approach for Evolutionary Multitasking

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3707341 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Evolutionary multitasking (EMT) algorithms typically require tailored designs for knowledge transfer, in order to assure convergence and optimality in multitask optimization. In this paper, we explore designing a systematic and generalizable knowledge transfer policy through Reinforcement Learning. We first identify three major challenges: determining the task to transfer (where), the knowledge to be transferred (what) and the mechanism for the transfer (how). To address these challenges, we formulate a multi-role RL system where three (groups of) policy networks act as specialized agents: a task routing agent incorporates an attention-based similarity recognition module to determine source-target transfer pairs via attention scores; a knowledge control agent determines the proportion of elite solutions to transfer; and a group of strategy adaptation agents control transfer strength by dynamically controlling hyper-parameters in the underlying EMT framework. Through pre-training all network modules end-to-end over an augmented multitask problem distribution, a generalizable meta-policy is obtained. Comprehensive validation experiments show state-of-the-art performance of our method against representative baselines. Further in-depth analysis not only reveals the rationale behind our proposal but also provide insightful interpretations on what the system have learned.

Multiform differential evolution with feasibility-oriented knowledge transfer for interval-constrained scheduling of coal-mine integrated energy systems

Canyun Dai; Xiaoyan Sun; Yong Zhang; Chen Ma; Dunwei Gong

OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Multiform differential evolution with feasibility-oriented knowledge transfer for interval-constrained scheduling of coal-mine integrated energy systems

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3707197 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

The scheduling of coal mine integrated energy systems confronts great challenges, such as high dimensionality, strong coupling constraints, multi-objective optimization and source-load uncertainties. Existing optimization methods struggle to simultaneously address the aforementioned challenges. We here propose a novel two-phase multiform differential evolution algorithm with feasibility-oriented knowledge transfer for scheduling coal mine integrated energy systems with source-load uncertainties, characterized by interval values. Within the proposed multiform optimization framework, the intricate energy coupling relationships are decomposed to formulate two alternative tasks: the electric-thermal task with uncertainty and electriccooling task with certainty. In the electric-thermal task, the objectives, constraints, and some optimization variables related to derived energy are represented as intervals. A feasibility-oriented knowledge transfer mechanism by exchanging the coupled electrical variables of the alternative tasks is developed to facilitate the knowledge sharing for effectively performing multiform evolutionary optimization. The proposed two-phase differential evolution incorporates different neighborhood dual-mutation mechanism. In the first phase, a diversity-driven mutation is employed to evolve the two alternative tasks and possibly explore the intersecting feasible regions. In the second phase, a convergence-driven mutation is implemented to refine the original task within the feasible space and obtain a set of well-distributed feasible Pareto solutions. The efficacy of the proposed algorithm is demonstrated through a case study conducted at a coal mine in Shanxi by comparing with six state-of-the-art constrained multi-objective evolutionary algorithms and an interval constrained multi-objective evolutionary algorithm. The results demonstrate the superior performance of the propose method in feasibility, convergence, diversity, and uncertainty handling.

Microscale-Searching Constrained Evolutionary Optimization for Diversity-Distribution-Guided Image Augmentation

Yueting Xu; Han Huang; Chaoda Peng; Ning Wu; Yun Liang; Qiong Huang

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Microscale-Searching Constrained Evolutionary Optimization for Diversity-Distribution-Guided Image Augmentation

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3706679 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Data augmentation serves as an effective technique for addressing data scarcity in image classification tasks. Existing approaches face fundamental limitations: diversity-oriented methods risk distributional deviation, while distribution-preserving methods typically neglect enhancing feature diversity. To simultaneously address the limitations, this paper formulates the data augmentation as a constrained optimization problem and proposes a constrained evolutionary algorithm to generate diverse augmented images that maintain distributional consistency with the original training data. The optimization formulation involves maximizing an objective function of feature diversity subject to a constraint of distributional consistency, with an image tensor as the decision variable. To efficiently solve this problem, we propose a microscale-searching constrained evolutionary algorithm that focuses computational resources on semantically critical regions by restricting the search space to neighborhoods around edge pixels of target objects. This space is dynamically expanded during evolution by incorporating neighborhoods around newly generated feasible solutions, collectively improving search efficiency while maintaining augmentation quality. Experimental results demonstrate that classifiers trained with the generated augmented images achieve superior accuracy compared to those using existing augmentation methods and conventional constrained evolutionary algorithms. This study extends evolutionary computation to image data augmentation through a constrained optimization framework with microscale searching.

EvoSpeak: Large Language Models for Interpretable Genetic Programming-Evolved Heuristics

Meng Xu; Jiao Liu; Yew Soon Ong

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3705492
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

EvoSpeak: Large Language Models for Interpretable Genetic Programming-Evolved Heuristics

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3705492 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

Genetic programming (GP) has demonstrated strong effectiveness in evolving tree-structured heuristics for complex optimization problems. Yet, in dynamic and large-scale scenarios, the most effective heuristics are often highly complex, hindering interpretability, slowing convergence, and limiting transferability across tasks. To address these challenges, we present EvoSpeak, a novel framework that integrates GP with large language models (LLMs) to enhance the efficiency, transparency, and adaptability of heuristic evolution. EvoSpeak learns from high-quality heuristics, extracts knowledge, and leverages this knowledge to (i) generate warm-start populations that accelerate convergence, (ii) translate opaque GP trees into concise natural-language explanations that foster interpretability and trust, and (iii) enable knowledge transfer and preference-aware heuristic generation across related tasks. We verify the effectiveness of EvoSpeak through extensive experiments on dynamic flexible job shop scheduling (DFJSS), under both single-and multi-objective formulations. The results demonstrate that EvoSpeak produces more effective heuristics, improves evolutionary efficiency, and delivers human-readable reports that enhance usability. By coupling the symbolic reasoning power of GP with the interpretative and generative strengths of LLMs, EvoSpeak advances the development of intelligent, transparent, and user-aligned heuristics for real-world optimization problems.

Rapidly Handling Constrained Multi-objective Optimization Problems with Hypervolume Gradient Subspace Approximation

Kenneth M. Zhang; Angel E. Rodriguez-Fernandez; Ke Shang; Hisao Ishibuchi; Oliver Schütze

卷期
V · I—
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tevc.2026.3704670
证据层级
OFFICIAL_DIRECTORY

官方论文页面ieeexplore.ieee.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-11-23 12:18 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Rapidly Handling Constrained Multi-objective Optimization Problems with Hypervolume Gradient Subspace Approximation

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation · DOI：10.1109/tevc.2026.3704670 · 摘要来源：已核验来源

In this paper, we propose a novel hybrid approach for the numerical treatment of constrained continuous multi-objective optimization problems (CMOPs). In the first step, we propose the set-based local search engine HVGSA that aims for improvements of the hypervolume (HV) indicator. The peculiarity of this operator is that its search direction can be computed without explicitly using gradient information or additional objective or HV calls (while a particular linear system of equations must be solved). Instead, existing neighborhood information is utilized, and the most greedy search direction out of the span of the resulting set of directions is approximated. As a by-product, the directional derivative along this direction can be approximated, which enables the use of adaptive step size controls. HVGSA is capable of handling both equality and inequality constraints and can, in principle, be applied to MOPs with any number of objectives. In the second step, we propose HVGSA-MOEA, a framework to hybridize (μ+μ) multi-objective evolutionary algorithms (MOEAs) with the new search engine. HVGSA can be applied either to entire populations or to particular subsets (elite individuals) in order to reduce the computational burden. We finally demonstrate the strength of HVGSA as a standalone algorithm and HVGSA-MOEA on real-world benchmark problems. The results indicate that the novel hybrid is highly competitive against the state-of-the-art.
